# Research Target

Let \(p\) be prime and let \(d\) be an integer satisfying \(100<d<p\). The dimension is fixed at \(m=2\). Degrees \(d\le 100\), including the degenerate case \(d=0\), are outside the campaign scope and must not be treated as obstructions to the target. A point table is a function

\[
f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p.
\]

For every affine line \(L\subseteq\mathbb F_p^2\), a line table supplies a univariate polynomial \(P_L\) of degree at most \(d\), interpreted on \(L\). Define the test agreement

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)
=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)],
\]

where \(L\) is uniform among affine lines and \(x\) is uniform on \(L\).

## Current benchmark

Kominers--Thaler--Zheng prove that there are absolute constants \(C,c>0\) such that

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)\ge C(d/p)^{1/3}
\]

implies the existence of a total-degree-at-most-\(d\) polynomial \(Q\) satisfying

\[
\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P).
\]

## Target theorem

The campaign seeks progress toward the following deliberately explicit research target.

For every fixed \(\eta>0\), prove constants \(C_\eta,c_\eta>0\) and a fully stated admissible parameter regime with \(100<d<p\) such that

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)
\ge C_\eta(d/p)^{1-\eta}
\]

implies

\[
\max_{\deg Q\le d}\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]
\ge c_\eta\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P).
\]

Equivalent or stronger list-decoding conclusions are admissible only when the conversion to a single polynomial and its loss are proved.

## Milestones

- **Bivariate exponent improvement:** replace \(1/3\) by an explicit \(\alpha>1/3\) over prime fields.
- **Combinatorial improvement:** strengthen the incidence/concentration stage with its full parameter range.
- **Algebraic improvement:** strengthen the interpolation/factorization stage with complete prime-characteristic handling.
- **Near-linear exponent:** prove \((d/p)^{1-\eta}\) for every fixed \(\eta>0\).
- **Sharp threshold:** identify matching examples or prove a theorem at \(\Theta(d/p)\), if true.

Because \(d/p<1\), a larger exponent is a stronger result: it permits smaller local agreement.

The general-dimensional lift is standard once the bivariate statement is known. It is a downstream corollary, not a research milestone, and the campaign spends no agents on it.

## Lemma writing rule

**A lemma statement contains only its quantified objects, hypotheses, and conclusion. It contains no motivation, derivation, commentary, proof sketch, interpretation, history, or explanation; all such material belongs in the proof.**

The dedicated Lemma Writer post-edits every structured proof step without changing its mathematical content or status. It may split a source lemma into ordered pieces when that makes the logical structure easier to understand. Raw submissions remain immutable, and every edited statement is source-linked and checked deterministically with KaTeX before publication in the Lemma Book.

## Proof roadmap policy

Three synchronized roadmap agents maintain complete dependency DAGs for the algebraic engine, incidence engine, and end-to-end soundness chain. They share all submissions, audits, Lemma Book entries, peer roadmaps, and informal messages; each gives a majority of its attention to its assigned route. Roadmap progress is harness-derived. A node is verified only when it cites an exact source job, proof-step id, and response hash whose claim was independently accepted, and all of its dependencies are verified or external inputs. Agent work states and message-board claims never confer verification.

## Non-results

The following do not count as achieving an exponent milestone:

- a theorem only for fixed \(d\), unless clearly labeled;
- a hidden assumption \(p\ge d^C\) that makes the stated threshold vacuous;
- a list of many global polynomials without a proved single-polynomial conclusion;
- an exponent obtained after suppressing characteristic- or logarithmic losses;
- numerical success on small fields;
- a proof for line-versus-line, plane-versus-point, or axis-parallel tests presented as a line-versus-point theorem without a proved reduction.
- a result whose substantive advance concerns \(m\ne2\), extension fields, or dimension bootstrapping rather than the bivariate prime-field theorem.

TITLE: The Bivariate Prime-Field Cubic Bottleneck: Support Sensitivity and a Modal Obstruction
STATUS: proved
THEOREM: For prime p, 101≤d<p, and any accepted carrier of edge mass δ supported on a σ-fraction of affine lines, δ³≥10^15dσ/p implies a total-degree-at-most-d polynomial with global agreement at least δ/8000. Under σ≤Aδ this yields a conditional exponent-1/2 threshold. Moreover, for d=101 and all sufficiently large primes there are modal constant line labels with ε=Θ(log p/(p log log p)) but Agr_101(f)<2/p; consequently no uniform trigger C d/p can guarantee global agreement cε for fixed positive C,c.
STEPS:
P1 | Prune an accepted carrier to minimum degree k0=floor(δp/100) without losing a power of δ.
P2 | Construct a support-sensitive selected line set.
P3 | Interpolate the selected line graphs at weighted degree D=O(sqrt(dσp/δ)).
P4 | Extend the interpolation identity and remove coefficient content safely.
P5 | A squarefree primitive polynomial of total degree below p is coprime to its vertical derivative.
P6 | Delete all degenerate roots with controlled loss.
P7 | Lift and propagate one total-degree-at-most-d polynomial on every surviving component.
P8 | Convert a component polynomial into global agreement δ/8000.
P9 | Identify the exact cubic exponent ledger.
P10 | Obtain exponent 1/2 under concentrated line support.
P11 | Lower-bound modal agreement on a random line.
P12 | Upper-control the modal local agreement.
P13 | Construct the deterministic modal table and bound every global polynomial.
P14 | Universal exact-linear soundness at threshold C d/p with linear recovery.
P15 | Every point table has global agreement at least (d+1)(d+2)/(2p²).
P16 | Diffuse accepted support need not imply a soundness obstruction or σ=O(ε).
P17 | Concentrated good directions alone furnish a counterexample.
P18 | Branch propagation remains valid without vertical simplicity.
P19 | Inseparability is an active obstruction in the audited interpolation range.

RESEARCHER-0004
TITLE: Uniform Normal Multiplicity Is Exponent-Neutral in the Bivariate KTZ Interpolation Step
STATUS: proved
THEOREM: For prime p, 100<d<p, distinct selected line graphs R of size n, multiplicity s>=1, and d<=D<p, order-s graph-ideal vanishing imposes at most n C_s(D,d) equations, where C_s(D,d)=sum_{a+b<s} max(D-a-db+1,0). If N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d), with N_d(D)=sum_{j<=D/d} binom(D-dj+2,2), a nonzero interpolant exists. Each compatible transverse intersection supplies an s-fold root, so h such points transfer the identity when sh>D. The explicit choice D_0=floor(4sqrt(dns(s+1)))+1 suffices when d<=D_0<p and D_0<sh. Conversely, in the KTZ regime n>D, any proof using only this raw dimension criterion and root count must satisfy h^2>dn/12. Thus at n=Theta(p/epsilon), h=Theta(epsilon p), uniform multiplicity retains the cubic condition epsilon^3=Omega(d/p).
STEPS:
P1 | Every lifted line graph has characteristic-safe normal coordinates preserving weighted degree.
P2 | Order-s graph vanishing costs exactly C_s(D,d) normal-jet channels on one line and at most nC_s across n lines.
P3 | The coefficient-versus-jet inequality suffices for interpolation.
P4 | Nontrivial Z-dependence and the primitive degree cost are fully quantified.
P5 | Full normal multiplicity transfers an s-fold root across one compatible intersection.
P6 | Multiplicity root counting transfers the relation to a target line.
P7 | An explicit finite-field interpolation-and-transfer parameter choice is valid.
P8 | Uniform multiplicity is exponent-neutral in the rank-blind KTZ interpolation/root-count architecture.

RESEARCHER-0005
TITLE: Hasse-Height Stratification and Factorwise Simple-Root Cleanup
STATUS: proved
THEOREM: For every prime p and integer 101<=d<p, a squarefree Z-dependent interpolant A of weighted degree a, with weights (1,1,d), admits an explicit factorwise cleanup. If A=HB, B=c product_i C_i, Delta=wdeg(B), and iota is the weighted degree of gcd(B,B_Z), then at most h+floor((Delta^2-dDelta+(d-1)iota)/d) content or exceptional graph lines are removed. Every remaining separable-type line loses at most min(p,Delta-d) points, every inseparable-type line at most min(p,Delta-1), and every retained point of graph degree at least two satisfies B_Z nonzero. Each irreducible factor has a p-power Hasse height s with weighted degree at least ds, and its height-s stratum obeys the common-graph bound (2r_s-s)Delta_s-d r_s^2. In particular, Delta<pd implies gcd(B,B_Z)=1; if a<=D<pd, at most floor(D^2/d-D) lines and D-d points per remaining line are lost. The threshold pd is sharp: Z^p-XY^(p-1) refutes any lifting rule based solely on multiplicity-weighted direction count. Under the quoted KTZ premise epsilon>=C_KTZ(d/p)^(1/3), the final global agreement remains epsilon/8000.
STEPS:

[P6] | The factorwise exceptional-line count is at most sum_i floor(delta_i(delta_i-omega_i)/d)+sum_{i<j} floor(delta_i delta_j/d), hence at most floor((Delta^2-dDelta+(d-1)iota)/d).
[P7] | After the exact per-line root deletion, the normalized loss is the theorem's content, exceptional-line, separable-root, and inseparable-root expression, with optional extra loss 1/(p+1).
[P8] | Every retained point incident to at least two retained graph lines is a simple vertical point of B.
[P9] | Weighted degree below pd guarantees vertical separability. For total weighted degree at most D<pd, at most floor(D^2/d-D) lines and D-d points per other line need be removed.
[P10] | Inserting the proved cleanup into the KTZ parameter envelope leaves the benchmark exponent 1/3 and final agreement epsilon/8000 unchanged.
[P11] | For every campaign pair p,d, a squarefree irreducible polynomial has multiplicity-weighted direction count p(p+1)>pd but no formal graph section.
[P12] | The purely inseparable pencil produces a counterexample to affine line-versus-point soundness.
[P13] | Concentration of all accepted incidences into a small set of directions alone obstructs global recovery.

RESEARCHER-0007
TITLE: Factorwise Weighted-Polar Accounting and a Sharp Whole-Relation Obstruction
STATUS: proved
THEOREM: For every prime p and integer 101≤d<p, let B∈F_p[X,Y,Z] be primitive in Z, squarefree, of positive Z-degree, and of (1,1,d)-weighted degree δ<p. Factor B=c∏C_i, put δ_i=wdeg(C_i), r_i=deg_Z(C_i), γ_i=wdeg(C_{i,Z}), and Xi_i=(r_i−1)δ_i+r_iγ_i−dr_i(r_i−1). A connected cleaned factor class of minimum degree k>δ_i with 1/sqrt(p+1)≤k/(4p) yields one total-degree-at-most-d polynomial with global agreement at least k/(4p). Conversely, for every 2d≤D<p there is an explicit legal table and primitive squarefree relation of weighted degree D for which the whole-relation polar loss is exactly (D−d)/(p+1)=τ(D−d)/p, with every intersection simple; for p≥8d this occurs at ε=Theta((d/p)^(1/3)). The construction has a factor Z with derivative one and a degree-zero global decoder, so it is a proof-architecture obstruction rather than a soundness counterexample.
STEPS:

P9 | The sharpness construction realizes the cubic parameter balance.
P10 | The sharpness construction forces KTZ's minimum-weight interpolant to incur the whole-relation polar loss.
P11 | Inseparability or concentrated good directions refute the factorwise theorem in the campaign regime.

RESEARCHER-0008
TITLE: Sharp Incidence-Only Losses in Bivariate Prime-Field Line–Point Agreement
STATUS: proved
THEOREM: Let p be prime, let 101≤d<p, fix m=2, and use uniform affine point-line sampling. There exist f:F_p²→F_p and degree-exactly-d line labels such that ε=d/p, every affine line has exactly d accepted points, every point has accepted degree d or d+1, exactly pd points have degree d+1, and every line supports acceptance. For every θ∈(0,1], the minimum total nonnegative line weight that gives every accepted incidence θ capped coverage through other accepted lines lies between θp(p+1)/(d+1) and θp(p+1)/(d−1); hence every integral such family has size Ω(p/ε). Nevertheless Q=0 has global agreement one. Moreover, for every valid table of agreement ε, the probability Γ₂ that two uniformly chosen distinct lines through a uniform point both accept there satisfies Γ₂=ε²−ε(1−ε)/p+Var_x(deg_acc(x))/[p(p+1)], and the lower bound is attained by accepting exactly k direction classes, ε=k/(p+1).
STEPS:
P1 | The affine point-line incidence graph admits a p+1-color decomposition in which every point sees all colors and each line misses one color.

P7 | The collision lower bound is attained by concentrated good directions for every ε=k/(p+1).

P10 | The quoted stronger prime-field incidence bounds do not supply linear component recovery within their own characteristic ranges.
P11 | Acceptance density alone always yields either more than quadratic collision energy or an o(p/ε)-weight universal reaching family.

RESEARCHER-0009
TITLE: Sharp Higher-Moment Envelopes and Raw-DRC Barriers for Good Affine Incidences
STATUS: proved
THEOREM: Fix a prime p, an integer 101<=d<p, and m=2. For every valid table of local agreement epsilon, if a_x and b_L are accepted point and line degrees and J_s is the adjacent-integer interpolation of k↦(k)_s, then Gamma_s^P>=J_s((p+1)epsilon)/(p+1)_s for 0<=s<=p+1 and Gamma_s^L>=J_s(p epsilon)/(p)_s for 0<=s<=p; with-replacement moments are at least epsilon^r. There is an explicit connected legal table at epsilon=d/p attaining both distinct-star envelopes simultaneously for every admissible s, with every line degree d and point degrees d or d+1. For every 0<theta<=1 its fractional other-line reaching optimum lies between theta p(p+1)/(d+1) and theta p(p+1)/(d-1), while Q=0 agrees globally everywhere. Every raw accepted graph is K_{2,2}-free. For the auxiliary line-compatibility graph H, an exact t-th degree-moment DRC bound and a concurrency correction yield at least M_t(H)-binom(t,2)(p+1)/(t-h+1) common-neighbor target lines with h distinct seed intersections.
STEPS:
P1 | For every valid table, Gamma_s^P=E_x[(a_x)_s]/(p+1)_s and Gamma_s^L=E_L[(b_L)_s]/(p)_s in their stated ranges.
P2 | For every valid table, the point and line distinct-star probabilities satisfy the J_s lower bounds, and both with-replacement probabilities are at least epsilon^r.
P3 | For every prime p and 101<=d<p, the explicit table has epsilon=d/p, every line degree d, exactly pd point vertices of degree d+1, and every other point degree d.
P4 | The accepted graph of the explicit table is connected and uses every point and affine line.
P5 | The explicit table simultaneously attains both distinct-star moment lower envelopes for every admissible order and attains the repeated line moment epsilon^r for every r>=1.
P6 | For every 0<theta<=1 on the explicit table, theta p(p+1)/(d+1)<=OPT_theta<=theta p(p+1)/(d-1).
P7 | Every accepted affine incidence graph is K_{2,2}-free, and two or more distinct vertices on either shore have at most one common neighbor.
P8 | For every valid table, e(H)=sum_x binom(a_x,2) and average_degree(H)>=p*mu(mu-1)/(p+1), where mu=(p+1)epsilon.
P9 | For every 1<=t<=floor(average_degree(H)), some distinct t-line seed set has at least M_t(H)>=N binom(floor(average_degree(H)),t)/binom(N,t) common neighbors; under the extra stated hypotheses this is at least N(epsilon^2/4)^t.
P10 | For every 1<=h<=t, the seed set in P9 has at least M_t(H)-binom(t,2)(p+1)/(t-h+1) common neighbors with at least h distinct seed-intersection points.
P11 | For fixed admissible d and t>256d, the acceptance-density-only auxiliary DRC certificate is asymptotically vacuous at the cubic scale as p tends to infinity.
P12 | Connectivity, full support, degree-moment extremality, and Theta(p/epsilon) universal reaching cost do not individually or jointly obstruct a single perfect global decoder.
P13 | The KTZ selector/interpolation/transfer chain reconstructs the benchmark exponent 1/3 and global agreement Omega(epsilon).

RESEARCHER-0010
TITLE: Popularity-Sensitive Core Decoding and a Bounded-Star-Energy Obstruction
STATUS: proved
THEOREM: For C_*=2^60, any nonempty accepted subgraph with point minimum degree u, line minimum degree v, line measure tau, and w=min(u,v), satisfying uv>=C_*dp and uw^2>=C_*d tau p^2, yields one total-degree-at-most-d Q with agreement at least v/(800p). Consequently, if the accepted-edge upper-tail threshold u_* satisfies u_*epsilon^2>=2^70d, then one Q has agreement at least epsilon/12800. The implication is proved, but its improved exponent requires an additional popularity hypothesis not known universally.
STEPS:
[P1] | Asymmetric mixing gives tau>=u/[4(p+1)], |X|/p^2>=v/(4p), and w^2>=2^57dp.
[P2] | There is a selector R of size Theta(t/u) reaching more than half of every target line's accepted points.
[P3] | A squarefree Z-dependent explainer A of weighted degree at most D exists, with D<min(u,v)/5000 and A_Z nonzero.
[P4] | The explainer identity extends from selected lines to every line in the accepted core.
[P5] | Prime-field cleanup produces a Z-primitive relation with simple retained roots after deleting at most pD^2+Dt edges.
[P6] | Asymmetric pruning preserves a nonempty simple-root graph with minima at least floor(u/100) and floor(v/100).
[P7] | One total-degree-at-most-d polynomial has global agreement at least v/(800p).
[P8] | A popularity tail u_*epsilon^2>=2^70d suffices; u_*>=kappa epsilon^a p gives the conditional exponent 1/(a+2).
[P9] | At most delta(p+1) algebraic templates carrying mass delta force one template with global agreement at least delta/4.
[P10] | Sufficient accepted mass on constant-labelled lines directly yields one popular constant global polynomial.
[P11] | All deterministic tables satisfy an exact falling-factorial star identity and its sharp degree-only lower bound.
[P12] | The modal construction has local agreement Theta(log p/(p log log p)) with explicit constants.
[P13] | A deterministic modal table simultaneously has baseline bounded-order energy and global degree-101 agreement below 2/p.
[P14] | A scale-free claim that lack of a decoder forces Gamma_s>=c epsilon^(s-gamma) is false.

/bin/zsh -lc "jq -r '.proof_steps[] | \"\\n### \" + .id + \"\\nSTATEMENT: \" + .statement + \"\\nDEPENDENCIES: \" + ((.dependencies // [])|join(\"; \")) + \"\\nSTATUS: \" + .status + \"\\nPROOF: \" + .proof' research_state/campaign-10-ultra/submissions/researcher-0010/response.json" in /Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point
 succeeded in 0ms:

### [P1]
STATEMENT: Asymmetric mixing gives tau>=u/[4(p+1)], |X|/p^2>=v/(4p), and w^2>=2^57dp.
DEPENDENCIES: KTZ Lemma 2.1
STATUS: proved
PROOF: Normalize point measure xi, line measure tau, and edge measure rho. Minimum degrees give rho>=u xi/(p+1) and rho>=v tau/p, while affine mixing gives rho<=xi tau+sqrt(xi tau/(p+1)). Assuming either tau<u/[4(p+1)] or xi<v/(4p) forces uv<2p. The theorem hypotheses contradict this. Substitution into the second gate yields w^2>=2^57dp.

### [P2]
STATEMENT: There is a selector R of size Theta(t/u) reaching more than half of every target line's accepted points.
DEPENDENCIES: [P1]; Chernoff bounds
STATUS: proved
PROOF: Sample each line at rate 8/u and define reach through R minus the target line. Along a fixed target line, the relevant random-line sets are disjoint, so reach indicators are independent. Failure is at most exp(-v/24). The bound w^2>=2^57dp makes the union over fewer than 2p^2 lines valid. Sample-size Chernoff bounds give 4t/u<=|R|<=16t/u.

### [P3]
STATEMENT: A squarefree Z-dependent explainer A of weighted degree at most D exists, with D<min(u,v)/5000 and A_Z nonzero.
DEPENDENCIES: [P2]; KTZ Lemmas 2.2, 2.3, and 2.5
STATUS: proved
PROOF: For D=16(floor(sqrt(d|R|))+1), one has D^2<=2^-45w^2, D<p, and |R|>=p>D. The characteristic-compatible monomial space has dimension exceeding |R|(D+1). A nonzero Z-dependent candidate exists; minimizing weighted degree among pointwise interpolants with nonzero Z-derivative gives a squarefree A. Since D<p, all selected pointwise identities are formal.

### [P4]
STATEMENT: The explainer identity extends from selected lines to every line in the accepted core.
DEPENDENCIES: [P2]; [P3]
STATUS: proved
PROOF: Every target line has more than v/2>D accepted points reached by another selected line. At each such point the two line values equal f. Thus the degree-at-most-D restriction of A has more than D roots and vanishes formally.

### [P5]
STATEMENT: Prime-field cleanup produces a Z-primitive relation with simple retained roots after deleting at most pD^2+Dt edges.
DEPENDENCIES: [P4]; KTZ Lemma 2.6
STATUS: proved
PROOF: Remove the Z-content H from A. The squarefree Z-primitive factor B has gcd(B,B_Z)=1 because deg_Z B<p. There are at most D content lines, D(D-1) resultant-degenerate lines, and D derivative-zero accepted points on every remaining line, totaling at most pD^2+Dt exceptional edges.

### [P6]
STATEMENT: Asymmetric pruning preserves a nonempty simple-root graph with minima at least floor(u/100) and floor(v/100).
DEPENDENCIES: [P1]; [P3]; [P5]
STATUS: proved
PROOF: The exceptional fraction is at most D/v+2^14dp/(uv)<1/4000. Peeling below floor(u/100) on points and floor(v/100) on lines costs at most |E|/50, so a nonempty graph remains with both minima above D.

### [P7]
STATEMENT: One total-degree-at-most-d polynomial has global agreement at least v/(800p).
DEPENDENCIES: [P1]; [P6]; KTZ Lemmas 6.1 and 6.3
STATUS: proved
PROOF: At any surviving point, more than D incident line roots and B_Z nonzero invoke KTZ lifting. Simple-root uniqueness propagates the same Q through a component. Asymmetric mixing gives that component point density at least floor(v/100)/(4p)>=v/(800p).

### [P8]
STATEMENT: A popularity tail u_*epsilon^2>=2^70d suffices; u_*>=kappa epsilon^a p gives the conditional exponent 1/(a+2).
DEPENDENCIES: [P7]
STATUS: conditional
PROOF: The u_* tail has normalized mass at least epsilon/2. Peeling at U=floor(u_*/2) and V=floor(delta_*p/4) leaves a core with U>=u_*/3, V>=epsilon p/16. The condition u_*epsilon^2>=2^70d verifies both gates of Theorem A and yields agreement epsilon/12800. The further hypothesis u_*>=kappa epsilon^a p translates this to exponent 1/(a+2).

### [P9]
STATEMENT: At most delta(p+1) algebraic templates carrying mass delta force one template with global agreement at least delta/4.
DEPENDENCIES: KTZ Lemma 2.1; Cauchy-Schwarz
STATUS: proved
PROOF: Apply affine mixing to each template's agreement set and line family. Sum the main terms and use Cauchy-Schwarz on the errors to obtain delta<=alpha tau+sqrt(R alpha tau/(p+1)). If R<=delta(p+1), alpha<delta/4 makes the right side less than delta.

### [P10]
STATEMENT: Sufficient accepted mass on constant-labelled lines directly yields one popular constant global polynomial.
DEPENDENCIES: [P9]
STATUS: proved
PROOF: For constant labels, the corresponding point agreement sets are disjoint, so the sum of their measures is one and the template-count loss disappears. This yields delta<=mu tau+sqrt(tau/(p+1)) and the stated decoding branch.

### [P11]
STATEMENT: All deterministic tables satisfy an exact falling-factorial star identity and its sharp degree-only lower bound.
DEPENDENCIES: 
STATUS: proved
PROOF: Condition on a point of accepted degree a_x and sample ordered distinct incident lines. This gives Gamma_s=E[(a_x)_s]/(p+1)_s. Discrete convexity yields the sharp interpolation between adjacent integer degrees; expanding s=2 gives the exact variance identity.

### [P12]
STATEMENT: The modal construction has local agreement Theta(log p/(p log log p)) with explicit constants.
DEPENDENCIES: Second-moment inequality; McDiarmid inequality
STATUS: proved
PROOF: For iid point values and independent symmetric modal ties, occupancy estimates give r/(65p)<=q<=17r/p. A one-point change alters epsilon by at most 1/p^2, so McDiarmid places realized epsilon in [q/2,3q/2] with failure at most 1/4.

### [P13]
STATEMENT: A deterministic modal table simultaneously has baseline bounded-order energy and global degree-101 agreement below 2/p.
DEPENDENCIES: [P12]; Chernoff bound; Markov inequality
STATUS: proved
PROOF: Conditioned on f(x), the punctured incident lines and symmetric tie coins are independent, giving E[Gamma_s]=q^s. Markov plus a union over fixed s costs 1/4. A union bound over p^5253 degree-101 polynomials costs at most p^5253(e/4)^p, and the line-maximum event costs at most 2p^3/v_0!. The remaining probability is positive.

### [P14]
STATEMENT: A scale-free claim that lack of a decoder forces Gamma_s>=c epsilon^(s-gamma) is false.
DEPENDENCIES: [P13]
STATUS: refuted
PROOF: In the modal tables, global agreement divided by epsilon tends to zero, but Gamma_s/epsilon^(s-gamma)=O_s(epsilon^gamma) also tends to zero. Hence the proposed implication fails for every fixed s, gamma, and positive constants.

  "note_markdown": "# Popularity-sensitive core decoding and a bounded-star-energy obstruction\n\n## Abstract\n\nFix a prime field $F_p$, dimension $m=2$, and an integer $100<d<p$. I prove an asymmetric refinement of the accepted-component decoder. If an accepted subgraph has point minimum degree $u$, line minimum degree $v$, line measure $τ$, and $w=min(u,v)$, then the two conditions\n\n$$\nuv \\ge 2^{60}dp,\n\\qquad\nuw^2 \\ge 2^{60}dτp^2\n$$\n\nforce one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial to agree with the point table on at least $v/(800p)$ of all points.\n\nFor the original accepted graph, let $u_*$ be the largest point-degree threshold supporting accepted-incidence mass at least $ε/2$. The component theorem implies\n\n$$\nu_*ε^2 \\ge 2^{70}d\n\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\nagr(f,Q) \\ge ε/12800.\n$$\n\nThus a hypothesis $u_*\\ge κ ε^a p$ gives the conditional threshold $ε\\ge(2^{70}/κ)^{1/(a+2)}(d/p)^{1/(a+2)}$. This interpolates from exponent $1/3$ at the universally available scale $u_*=Ω(εp)$ to exponent $1/2$ when $u_*=Ω(p)$. It does not improve the unconditional benchmark.\n\nI also prove a template-popularity lemma and a complementary obstruction: for every fixed star order, diffuse modal-constant tables can have star energy only $O(ε^s)$ while every degree-101 global polynomial has agreement $o(ε)$. Consequently, a scale-free bounded-order collision-energy increment cannot supply the missing popularity.\n\n## Test and Notation\n\nThere are $p^2$ points and $p(p+1)$ affine lines. Every point lies on $p+1$ lines and every line contains $p$ points. Each affine line $L$ carries a univariate polynomial $P_L$ of degree at most the ambient integer $d$, and $f:F_p^2\\to F_p$ is the point table. The sampling rule is exactly a uniformly random affine line followed by a uniformly random point on it; equivalently, it is a uniform affine incidence. Put\n\n$$\nε=Pr_{L,\\,x\\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)].\n$$\n\nThe accepted graph has edge $(x,L)$ exactly when $x\\in L$ and $P_L(x)=f(x)$. All global polynomials below have total degree at most $d$.\n\nFor a subgraph $G=(X,T,E)$, let\n\n$$\nt=|T|,\n\\qquad\nτ=\\frac{t}{p(p+1)},\n$$\n\nand let $u$ and $v$ denote its point-side and line-side minimum degrees, respectively. For the full accepted graph, write $a_x$ for the accepted degree of $x$ and define the absolute upper-tail incidence mass\n\n$$\nδ_j=\\frac{1}{p^2(p+1)}\\sum_{x:a_x\\ge j}a_x,\n\\qquad\nu_*=max\\{j\\in\\{1,\\ldots,p+1\\}:δ_j\\ge ε/2\\}.\n$$\n\nWhen $ε>0$, this set is nonempty. Since points below $ε(p+1)/2$ carry less than half of all accepted edges,\n\n$$\nu_*\\ge \\left\\lceil\\frac{ε(p+1)}2\\right\\rceil.\n$$\n\nFor $s\\ge2$, $Γ_s$ denotes the probability that $s$ ordered distinct uniformly sampled lines through a uniform point all accept there.\n\n## Prior Results\n\nThe exact benchmark is Theorem 1.1 of [Kominers--Thaler--Zheng, ECCC TR26-147, Revision 1](https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2026/147/revision/1/download/): over every finite field, $ε\\ge C(d/p)^{1/3}$ implies one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial with agreement at least $cε$. The following explanation of the exponent is my reconstruction, not a separately quoted theorem: their Lemma 3.2 gives $|R|=O(p/ε)$, Lemma 2.2 gives an explainer degree $D=O(\\sqrt{dp/ε})$, and identity extension and lifting require $D=O(εp)$. Hence $ε^3=Ω(d/p)$.\n\nThe present proof uses KTZ Revision 1, Lemmas 2.1--2.3, 2.5--2.7, 6.1, and 6.3. The local cached KTZ file is the earlier July 2026 version; theorem numbering is stable, but the revision above is the pinned source. The cached Arora--Sudan file is the STOC 1997 version rather than the Combinatorica 2003 article recorded in the bibliography, and no theorem-number transfer from that cache is used.\n\nThe durable corpus was audited through [DATA_MANIFEST.json](/Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point/research_state/DATA_MANIFEST.json), all eight active campaign-10 submissions, their verifier audits, all active leaderboards, and the empty campaign-300 leaderboards; directories named `superseded` were excluded. The main reusable prior result is the verifier-accepted symmetric component theorem in [researcher-0002](/Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point/research_state/campaign-10-ultra/submissions/researcher-0002/note.md). The exact pair-collision and regular-carrier obstruction in [researcher-0008](/Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point/research_state/campaign-10-ultra/submissions/researcher-0008/note.md) shows that graph regularity alone gives no energy surplus. The verified singular-lift obstruction in [researcher-0006](/Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point/research_state/campaign-10-ultra/submissions/researcher-0006/note.md) remains relevant to any future factorwise refinement.\n\nFor historical comparison only, Theorem 4.2 of [Harsha--Kumar--Saptharishi--Sudan, arXiv v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12752) states the bivariate condition $p>Cd/ε^7$. It is not used in the proof.\n\n## Theorem\n\n**Theorem A (proved asymmetric core decoder).** Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let $G=(X,T,E)$ be a nonempty accepted subgraph. Suppose every $x\\in X$ has degree at least the integer $u$, every $L\\in T$ has degree at least the integer $v$, $τ=|T|/[p(p+1)]$, and $w=min(u,v)$. If\n\n$$\nuv\\ge2^{60}dp,\n\\qquad\nuw^2\\ge2^{60}dτp^2,\n$$\n\nthen there is a single $Q\\in F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying\n\n$$\nPr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge\\frac{v}{800p}.\n$$\n\nConnectedness of the input is not required.\n\n**Corollary B (proved implication; conditional exponent).** With $u_*$ defined above, if\n\n$$\nu_*ε^2\\ge2^{70}d,\n$$\n\nthen some total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial satisfies $agr(f,Q)\\ge ε/12800$. More generally, if $0\\le a\\le1$, $0<κ\\le1$, and $u_*\\ge κ ε^a p$, then the conclusion holds whenever\n\n$$\nε\\ge\\left(\\frac{2^{70}}κ\\right)^{1/(a+2)}\n       \\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1/(a+2)}.\n$$\n\nThe exponent improvement for $a<1$ is conditional because no such popularity tail is known for every table.\n\n**Lemma C (proved template popularity).** Let $T_1,\\ldots,T_R$ be disjoint line families and let $Q_1,\\ldots,Q_R$ be total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomials such that $P_L=Q_j|_L$ for $L\\in T_j$. Let $τ_j=|T_j|/[p(p+1)]$, $τ=\\sum_jτ_j$, let $δ>0$ be the normalized accepted-incidence mass on their union, and put $α=max_j agr(f,Q_j)$. Then\n\n$$\nδ\\le ατ+\\sqrt{\\frac{Rατ}{p+1}}.\n$$\n\nIn particular, $R\\leδ(p+1)$ implies $α\\geδ/4$.\n\n**Proposition D (proved bounded-star-energy obstruction).** Fix $S\\ge2$. For all sufficiently large primes $p$, at the legal ambient degree $d=101$ there is a deterministic table using constant line polynomials such that, for\n\n$$\nr=\\left\\lfloor\\frac{\\log p}{4\\log\\log p}\\right\\rfloor,\n$$\n\none has\n\n$$\n\\frac{r}{130p}\\le ε\\le\\frac{26r}{p},\n\\qquad\nmax_{deg Q\\le101}agr(f,Q)<\\frac2p,\n$$\n\nand, simultaneously for $2\\le s\\le S$,\n\n$$\nΓ_s\\le2^{s+2}(S-1)ε^s.\n$$\n\nA fully explicit sufficient meaning of sufficiently large is obtained by setting $u_0=\\lceil4\\log p/\\log\\log p\\rceil$ and $v_0=\\lceil8\\log p/\\log\\log p\\rceil$ and requiring\n\n$$\nr\\ge133,\n\\quad r^2\\le p,\n\\quad p\\ge8r!,\n\\quad u_0+1\\le17r,\n\\quad v_0\\le33r,\n$$\n\n$$\n\\frac{2p}{(u_0+1)!}\\le1,\n\\quad\n\\frac{2p^3}{v_0!}\\le\\frac18,\n\\quad\np^{5253}(e/4)^p\\le\\frac18,\n\\quad\nS\\le p+1.\n$$\n\n## Proof or Conditional Proof\n\n**[P1] (proved: asymmetric mixing).** Put $ξ=|X|/p^2$, $ρ=|E|/[p^2(p+1)]$, $α_0=u/(p+1)$, $β_0=v/p$, and $λ=(p+1)^{-1/2}$. Minimum degree and KTZ Lemma 2.1 give\n\n$$\nα_0ξ\\leρ,\n\\qquad\nβ_0τ\\leρ,\n\\qquad\nρ\\leξτ+λ\\sqrt{ξτ}.\n$$\n\nIf $τ<α_0/4$, the first and third inequalities imply $ξ<(16/9)λ^2τ/α_0^2$. Substitution into the third inequality and comparison with $β_0τ\\leρ$ give $α_0β_0<2λ^2$, or $uv<2p$. This contradicts the first hypothesis. The symmetric calculation gives\n\n$$\nτ\\ge\\frac{u}{4(p+1)},\n\\qquad\nξ\\ge\\frac{v}{4p}.\n$$\n\nThus $t\\ge up/4$. Substituting the line-measure bound into the second hypothesis yields\n\n$$\nw^2\\ge\\frac{2^{60}dp^2}{4(p+1)}\\ge2^{57}dp.\n$$\n\nThis last inequality, rather than an unstated fixed-$d$ assumption, supplies all logarithmic room below.\n\n**[P2] (proved: selector and probability losses).** The first hypothesis and $v\\le p$ give $u>8$. Select every line of $T$ independently with probability $8/u$. A point accepted on a target line $L$ is called reached only through a selected line in $R$ other than $L$. It has at least $u-1$ eligible lines, so its reach probability exceeds $3/4$. For distinct points of $L$, the eligible line sets are disjoint because two distinct points determine their unique common line, which is the excluded line $L$. Chernoff therefore bounds the probability that at most half of $N_G(L)$ is reached by $e^{-v/24}$.\n\nSince $v\\ge w\\ge\\sqrt{2^{57}dp}$, $2p^2e^{-v/24}<1/4$; this accounts explicitly for the union bound over all target lines. The sample-size mean is $8t/u\\ge2p$. Its lower- and upper-tail Chernoff bounds are respectively at most $e^{-p/4}$ and $e^{-2p/3}$. Hence some choice has\n\n$$\n\\frac{4t}{u}\\le r=|R|\\le\\frac{16t}{u}\n$$\n\nand reaches more than half of every line's accepted points. In particular, $r\\ge p$.\n\n**[P3] (proved: interpolation).** Set\n\n$$\nD=16(\\lfloor\\sqrt{dr}\\rfloor+1).\n$$\n\nThen $D^2>256dr$ and\n\n$$\nD^2\\le1024dr\n \\le2^{14}\\frac{dt}{u}\n \\le2^{15}\\frac{dτp^2}{u}\n \\le2^{-45}w^2.\n$$\n\nThus $D<w/5000$, $D<p$, and $r\\ge p>D$. KTZ Lemma 2.2 gives $dim V_D\\ge D^3/(64d)>r(D+1)$. Each formal identity on a selected line costs at most $D+1$ homogeneous conditions, so a nonzero candidate in $V_D$ exists. If it were Z-independent, KTZ Lemma 2.5 and $r>D$ would force it to vanish identically. Its allowed positive Z-exponents are not divisible by $p$, so its Z-derivative is nonzero.\n\nLet $S_R$ be the finite union of the selected polynomial graphs and choose a nonzero polynomial $A$ of minimum weighted degree among polynomials vanishing on $S_R$ with $A_Z\\ne0$. KTZ Lemma 2.3 makes $A$ squarefree. Its weight is at most $D<p$, so pointwise vanishing at all $p$ parameters on a selected line is a formal identity. This also explains why repeated-factor removal cannot lose the line identities.\n\n**[P4] (proved: identity extension).** Every $L\\in T$ has more than $v/2>D$ accepted points reached through a selected $M\\ne L$. At such a point,\n\n$$\nP_L(x)=f(x)=P_M(x),\n$$\n\nso $A(x,P_L(x))=0$. The restricted polynomial $A(L(t),P_L(t))$ has degree at most $D$ and more than $D$ roots; hence it vanishes formally. There is no Markov or multiplicity loss at this step.\n\n**[P5] (proved: prime-field simple-root cleanup).** Write $A=HB$, where $H(X,Y)$ is the gcd of the Z-coefficients of $A$. At most $D$ affine lines are contained in $H=0$. On every other line, division in the integral domain $F_p[t]$ gives $B(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$. The polynomial $B$ is squarefree, Z-primitive, Z-dependent, and has degree below $p$.\n\nMoreover, $gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. Indeed, if an irreducible $C$ divided both, squarefreeness and reduction of $(CE)_Z$ modulo $C$ would give $C|C_Z$, hence $C_Z=0$. Since $deg_Z C<p$, this makes $C$ Z-independent, contradicting Z-primitivity. KTZ Lemma 2.6 now bounds the lines on whose graphs both $B$ and $B_Z$ vanish by $D(D-1)$. On every other line, $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))$ is a nonzero polynomial of degree at most $D$, so it has at most $D$ accepted roots. Thus at most\n\n$$\npD^2+Dt\n$$\n\naccepted edges are deleted. No discriminant or derivative is divided by.\n\n**[P6] (proved: asymmetric pruning).** Since $|E|\\ge vt$,\n\n$$\n\\frac{pD^2+Dt}{|E|}\n \\le\\frac Dv+\\frac{pD^2}{vt}\n \\le\\frac Dv+\\frac{2^{14}dp}{uv}\n <\\frac1{4000}.\n$$\n\nLet $u_1=\\lfloor u/100\\rfloor$ and $v_1=\\lfloor v/100\\rfloor$. Iteratively delete point vertices of current degree below $u_1$ and line vertices below $v_1$. This costs less than\n\n$$\nu_1|X|+v_1|T|\\le |E|/50.\n$$\n\nA nonempty graph remains. The hypotheses imply $u_1\\ge u/200$, $v_1\\ge v/200$, and $u_1,v_1>D$.\n\n**[P7] (proved: lift, propagate, recover measure).** Every surviving point is incident to an identity line and hence satisfies $B(x,f(x))=0$; retained edges also certify $B_Z(x,f(x))\\ne0$. At a point in any surviving connected component, more than $D$ incident line roots satisfy the hypotheses of KTZ Lemma 6.1. It produces one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $Q$ with $B(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\\equiv0$. The one-variable simple-root uniqueness used in KTZ Lemma 6.3 propagates this same $Q$ through the component.\n\nSince $u_1v_1\\ge uv/40000>2p$, [P1]'s asymmetric mixing calculation applied to this component gives point density at least\n\n$$\n\\frac{v_1}{4p}\\ge\\frac{v}{800p}.\n$$\n\nThe polynomial agrees with $f$ at every point of that component, proving Theorem A. There is no list-size or factor-count loss.\n\n**[P8] (conditional: popularity-tail translation).** Let $δ_*=δ_{u_*}\\geε/2$ and retain all accepted edges incident to points of degree at least $u_*$. Their number is $E_*=δ_*p^2(p+1)$. Peel point degrees below $U=\\lfloor u_*/2\\rfloor$ and line degrees below $V=\\lfloorδ_*p/4\\rfloor$. Point deletions cost at most $E_*/2$, and line deletions cost at most\n\n$$\nVp(p+1)\\le E_*/4.\n$$\n\nThus a nonempty core remains. Under $u_*ε^2\\ge2^{70}d$, floors give\n\n$$\nU\\ge u_*/3,\n\\qquad\nV\\geδ_*p/8\\geεp/16,\n\\qquad\nmin(U,V)\\geεp/16.\n$$\n\nConsequently,\n\n$$\nUV\\ge u_*εp/48>2^{60}dp\n$$\n\nand, for every component line measure $τ_C\\le1$,\n\n$$\nUmin(U,V)^2\n \\ge u_*ε^2p^2/768\n >2^{60}dτ_Cp^2.\n$$\n\nTheorem A gives agreement at least $V/(800p)\\geε/12800$. If $u_*\\geκ ε^a p$, the displayed hypothesis follows from $κ ε^{a+2}p\\ge2^{70}d$. Universally $u_*\\geεp/2$, so this recovers exponent $1/3$ with a large constant. For $a<1$ it is a genuine conditional improvement; $a=0$ gives exponent $1/2$.\n\n**[P9] (proved: template popularity).** Put $S_j=\\{x:Q_j(x)=f(x)\\}$ and $α_j=|S_j|/p^2$. The carried edges in $T_j$ are exactly the incidences between $S_j$ and $T_j$. KTZ Lemma 2.1 gives\n\n$$\nδ\\le\\sum_jα_jτ_j+\n\\frac1{\\sqrt{p+1}}\\sum_j\\sqrt{α_jτ_j}.\n$$\n\nThe first term is at most $ατ$. Cauchy--Schwarz, the only loss here, bounds the second sum by $\\sqrt{Rατ}$. This proves Lemma C. If $R\\leδ(p+1)$ and $α<δ/4$, the two right-hand terms are below $δ/4$ and $δ/2$, a contradiction.\n\n**[P10] (proved: constant-label branch).** For constant-labelled lines, group by their constant value $a$. The sets $\\{x:f(x)=a\\}$ are disjoint, so Cauchy--Schwarz uses $\\sum_aPr[f=a]=1$ and removes the $R$ factor:\n\n$$\nδ\\le μτ+\\sqrt{\\frac{τ}{p+1}},\n\\qquad\nμ=max_aPr[f=a].\n$$\n\nThus $δ\\ge2\\sqrt{τ/(p+1)}$ implies $μ\\geδ/(2τ)\\geδ/2$. If constant lines carry at least a $θ$ fraction of the total agreement, then $ε\\ge2/[θ\\sqrt{p+1}]$ already yields a constant global polynomial of agreement at least $θε/2$.\n\n**[P11] (proved: exact star moments).** For every deterministic table,\n\n$$\nΓ_s=\\frac{1}{p^2(p+1)_s}\\sum_x(a_x)_s.\n$$\n\nThe integer sequence $(n)_s$ is discretely convex. If $μ=ε(p+1)=k+θ$ with integer $k=\\lfloor μ\\rfloor$ and $0\\leθ<1$, convexity gives the sharp degree-only lower bound\n\n$$\nΓ_s\\ge\n\\frac{(1-θ)(k)_s+θ(k+1)_s}{(p+1)_s}.\n$$\n\nFor $s=2$, expansion yields the exact identity\n\n$$\nΓ_2=ε^2-\\frac{ε(1-ε)}p+\n\\frac{Var_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.\n$$\n\nThus even perfectly regular accepted degrees supply only baseline collision energy $Θ(ε^2)$.\n\n**[P12] (proved: modal occupancy and concentration).** Choose every value $f(x)$ independently and uniformly. On each line choose uniformly among its modal values, using fresh independent line coins, and use that value as the constant polynomial $P_L$. Symmetric independent tie-breaking is essential; arbitrary deterministic tie-breaking need not give the claimed star identity.\n\nLet $M$ be the maximum occupancy when $p$ balls are thrown into $p$ bins and let $q=E[M]/p=E[ε]$. If $Y$ counts bins of occupancy exactly $r$, the explicit conditions in Proposition D give\n\n$$\nE[Y]\\ge\\frac{p}{8r!}\\ge1,\n\\qquad\nE[Y^2]\\le E[Y]+\\frac{p^2}{(r!)^2}\\le65E[Y]^2.\n$$\n\nThe second-moment inequality gives $Pr[M\\ge r]\\ge1/65$, hence $q\\ge r/(65p)$. Conversely, $Pr[M\\ge s]\\le p/s!$, so the stated factorial condition gives $E[M]\\le u_0+1\\le17r$.\n\nChanging one point value changes maxima on only its $p+1$ incident lines and changes $ε$ by at most $1/p^2$. McDiarmid therefore gives\n\n$$\nPr[|ε-q|>q/2]\n \\le2exp(-q^2p^2/2)\n \\le2exp(-r^2/8450)\n \\le1/4.\n$$\n\n**[P13] (proved: simultaneous low energy and no decoder).** Fix a point and condition on its value. The punctured incident lines partition all other points. Their values and the independent tie coins are independent. Label symmetry and double counting the $M$ modal positions show that each incident line accepts the point with probability exactly $q$. Hence $a_x$ is conditionally binomial and $E[Γ_s]=q^s$.\n\nMarkov at threshold $4(S-1)q^s$, followed by a union bound over $2\\le s\\le S$, fails with probability at most $1/4$. On $ε\\ge q/2$, this gives $Γ_s\\le2^{s+2}(S-1)ε^s$.\n\nFor each fixed total-degree-at-most-101 polynomial $Q$, its agreement count with random $f$ is $Bin(p^2,1/p)$, so\n\n$$\nPr[agr(f,Q)\\ge2/p]\\le(e/4)^p.\n$$\n\nThere are $p^{5253}$ candidates because $(101+1)(101+2)/2=5253$. Their union-bound failure is at most $1/8$. The probability that any line has modal occupancy at least $v_0$ is at most $2p^3/v_0!\\le1/8$. All four bad-event probabilities total at most $3/4$, so a deterministic realization exists after freezing both $f$ and the tie coins. It has $r/(130p)\\leε\\le26r/p$, every line supports acceptance, every line accepts fewer than $v_0\\le33r$ points, the stated star bounds, and no global decoder of agreement $2/p$.\n\n**[P14] (refuted claim; obstruction proved).** Fix $s\\ge2$, $γ>0$, and constants $c_0,c_1>0$. The scale-free assertion\n\n$$\nmax_{deg Q\\le101}agr(f,Q)\\le c_0ε\n\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\nΓ_s\\ge c_1ε^{s-γ}\n$$\n\nis false. Proposition D has global-to-local ratio below $260/r\\to0$, while $Γ_s=O_s(ε^s)=o(ε^{s-γ})$. This does not refute a target-range energy lemma: for every fixed $η>0$,\n\n$$\n\\frac{ε}{(101/p)^{1-η}}\n =O_η\\left(\\frac{r}{p^η}\\right)\\to0.\n$$\n\n## Exponent Ledger\n\n| Stage | Input scale | Loss | Output scale | Status |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| KTZ reconstruction | $|R|=O(p/ε)$ | $D=O(\\sqrt{dp/ε})$ and $D=O(εp)$ | $ε^3=Ω(d/p)$ | proved |\n| Asymmetric selector | $t=τp(p+1)$, point degree $u$ | Chernoff and one union bound; constants only | $r=Θ(τp^2/u)$ | proved |\n| Interpolation | $r$ line identities | $D=Θ(\\sqrt{dr})$ | $D=O(\\sqrt{dτp^2/u})$ | proved |\n| Identity/lift | roots $v$ and incident lines $u$ | require $D<min(u,v)$ | $u min(u,v)^2=Ω(dτp^2)$ | proved |\n| Cleanup | $pD^2+Dt$ bad edges | relative $D/v+O(dp/(uv))$ | $uv=Ω(dp)$ | proved |\n| Recovery | minima $u/100,v/100$ | affine mixing constant | agreement $Ω(v/p)$ | proved |\n| Popularity core | $u_*$ and mass $ε/2$ | $U=Ω(u_*),V=Ω(εp)$ | $u_*ε^2=Ω(d)$ | conditional |\n| Exponent translation | $u_*\\geκ ε^a p$ | none beyond constants | $ε=Ω((d/p)^{1/(a+2)})$ | conditional |\n| Template branch | $R,δ,τ$ | $\\sqrt{Rατ/p}$ by Cauchy--Schwarz | $R\\leδp$ gives $α=Ω(δ)$ | proved |\n| Modal obstruction | $ε=Θ(r/p)$ | fixed-order Markov constants | $Γ_s=O_s(ε^s)$ and global $<2/p$ | proved |\n\nBecause $d/p<1$, the conditional exponent $1/(a+2)$ is stronger than $1/3$ exactly when $a<1$. Its largest value in this architecture is $1/2$, not the long-term $1-o(1)$ target.\n\n## Counterexample Attempts\n\nThe modal table is diffuse: every affine line in every direction supports acceptance, no line accepts more than $O(\\log p/\\log\\log p)$ points, and yet every bounded star moment stays at its baseline scale. It rules out energy increments based only on a fixed number of coincident accepted lines.\n\nThe regular Hall carrier from the active corpus has full line support and pair energy $Θ(ε^2)$ but a perfect constant decoder. Together, the two examples show that low collision energy occurs in both easy and hard tables. An effective dichotomy must inspect algebraic coherence, not merely degrees or support.\n\nConcentrating all good lines in $k$ directions gives $ε=k/(p+1)$ and $Γ_s=(k)_s/(p+1)_s$, but the planted global polynomial is immediate. Concentrated directions therefore do not refute the theorem.\n\nAttempts based on $t^p-t$, Frobenius aliases, or inseparable line functions require degree at least $p$ and violate $d<p$. At the lower legal boundary, Proposition D uses ambient degree $d=101$; its constant line labels are allowed under that cap. For $d$ close to $p$, triangular interpolation gives every point table agreement at least $(d+1)(d+2)/(2p^2)$, exceeding $1/8$ once $d\\ge(p-1)/2$. In particular, at $(p,d)=(103,101)$ it is $5253/10609\\approx0.495$.\n\nThese are analytic falsification checks. No finite computation is used to prove an asymptotic soundness statement.\n\n## Characteristic Audit\n\nAll statements are restricted to the prime field. The proof enforces $D<p$ before any functional-to-formal conversion. The line labels have degree $d<p$, and the restricted explainer identities have degree at most $D<p$.\n\nThe interpolation space excludes positive Z-exponents divisible by the characteristic; in this prime-field parameter range, $D<p$ also prevents them directly. Minimum-weight interpolation is invoked only after a candidate with nonzero Z-derivative is constructed. Squarefreeness is therefore justified by the exact hypotheses of KTZ Lemma 2.3.\n\nAfter Z-content removal, primitivity plus squarefreeness and $deg_ZB<p$ prove $gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. This directly handles irreducibility and inseparability; no generic derivative, discriminant division, or unverified separability assertion is needed. KTZ Lemma 2.6 applies its resultant only to this certified coprime pair. Newton lifting divides solely by a value of $B_Z$ retained as nonzero.\n\nOrdinary multiplicity-one interpolation is used throughout. The selector's only union loss is $2p^2e^{-v/24}$, the modal star argument's Markov-plus-union loss is $1/4$, the global-polynomial union loss is $p^{5253}(e/4)^p$, and the only Cauchy--Schwarz loss in the template lemma is $\\sqrt{Rατ/(p+1)}$.\n\n## Limitations\n\nNo unconditional popularity estimate stronger than $u_*=Ω(εp)$ is proved. Substituting that universal estimate into Corollary B recovers the cubic benchmark and nothing more, so `benchmark_improved=false`.\n\nEven perfect point popularity $u_*=Θ(p)$ yields only exponent $1/2$ in this architecture because the simple-root cleanup and identity/lifting gates remain. Progress toward exponent $1-o(1)$ must replace those interfaces, not merely iterate the present popularity pruning.\n\nThe modal obstruction is scale-free and lies below every fixed target trigger. It does not rule out an energy increment whose hypothesis explicitly uses $ε\\gtrsim(d/p)^{1-η}$, whose order grows with $p$, or whose energy records algebraic templates. Lemma C is useful only after such templates have actually been extracted; it does not produce them.\n\nThe constants are intentionally conservative and make Theorem A vacuous for modest fields. The result concerns only $m=2$, prime fields, total degree, and uniform affine-incidence sampling. No dimension lift, extension-field descent, or list-only conclusion is asserted."
}

If $$k^2\ge2^{40}dp,\qquad k^3\ge2^{40}d\tau p^2,$$ then there is one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{k}{800p}.$$
AE-06 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-05 :: Whole-explainer square-root floor :: The AE-05 construction has $$|R|=\Theta\left(\frac{\tau p^2}{k}\right),\qquad D=\Theta\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\tau p^2}{k}}\right),\qquad D<k.$$ Together with $\tau\ge k/(4p)$, this forces $k^2=\Omega(dp)$ and hence cannot reach $k=\Theta(\epsilon p)$ below $\epsilon=\Omega((d/p)^{1/2})$.
AE-07 [theorem/candidate] deps=AE-01 :: Universal-cover obstruction :: For suitable legal instances with $1/(2K)\le\epsilon\le2/K$, every line family reaching half the accepted points of every accepted line has size at least $p/(32\epsilon)$, although a constant polynomial has agreement at least $\epsilon/2$.
AE-08 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-02 :: Uniform-jet rank barrier :: Let $s,D\in\mathbb Z$ with $s\ge1$, $d\le D<p$, and $n>D$. Define $$C_s(D,d)=\sum_{a+b<s}\max(D-a-db+1,0).$$ If raw dimension counting gives $N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d)$ and transfer uses $h$ compatible intersections with $sh>D$, then $$h^2>\frac{dn}{12}.$$
AE-09 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-02 :: Characteristic-safe separability and resultants :: If $B$ is squarefree and primitive in $Z$ with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(B)<pd$, then $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. If coprime $U,V$ have weighted degrees $u,v$ and $Z$-degrees $r,s$, respectively, then the number of graph lines on which both vanish identically is at most $$su+rv-drs.$$
AE-10 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-09 :: Assigned-factor polar cleanup :: Let $B=c\prod_iC_i$ be primitive and squarefree with $\operatorname{wdeg}(B)<p$, and assign each graph line to one factor. Put $$\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),\quad r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),\quad\gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),$$ and $$\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).$$ If $T_i$ is the assigned line class and $b_i$ is its number of identically ramified lines, then $$b_i\le\min\{|T_i|,\Xi_i\},\qquad\gamma_i\le\delta_i-d,\qquad\Xi_i\le\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i.$$ The ramified accepted incidences satisfy $$\frac{|E_{\mathrm{ram}}|}{p^2(p+1)}\le\sum_i\frac{\min\{|T_i|,\Xi_i\}}{p(p+1)}+\sum_i\frac{|T_i|\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}.$$
AE-11 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-09 :: Singular-seed obstruction :: For legal choices with $d+1<p$, there is a primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible polynomial $$C=XZ+H(Y),\qquad\operatorname{wdeg}(C)=d+1,$$ supporting more than $d+1$ compatible degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines through one common lift, but no $Q$ of total degree at most $d$ with $C(X,Y,Q)=0$. At the common lift, $C_Z=0$.
AE-12 [obligation/open] deps=AE-03 :: Bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy :: For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$, there are constants $C_\theta,a_\theta>0$ and $K_\theta\ge1$ such that, whenever $$e(\mathcal I)=\epsilon\ge C_\theta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\theta},$$ either one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $\Pr[Q=f]\ge a_\theta\epsilon$, or there is a rank-reaching certificate $(G,R,D)$ with $$e(G)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,\qquad d\le D\le K_\theta d.$$
AE-13 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-12 :: Near-linear parameter gate :: After increasing $C_\theta$ in AE-12, the relation alternative satisfies $$\frac{D}{\epsilon p}\le\frac{K_\theta}{C_\theta}\left(\frac dp\right)^\theta\le\frac{a_\theta}{100}.$$ Consequently, $$D<p,\qquad \left(\frac Dp\right)^2+\frac{3D}{p}\le\frac{a_\theta\epsilon}{2}.$$
AE-14 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-13 :: Squarefree rank-kernel relation :: In the relation alternative, there is a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ with $$\rho_M(A)=0\quad(M\in R),\qquad A_Z\ne0.$$ Indeed, take a nonzero kernel element and replace it by the product of its distinct irreducible factors. The inequality $|R|>D$ excludes a $Z$-independent relation, while $\deg_Z(A)\le D/d<p$ makes $Z$-dependence characteristic-safe.
AE-15 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-14 :: Reached-root identity extension :: For every line $L$ of $G$, $$\rho_L(A)=A(L(t),P_L(t))=0.$$ Each reached accepted point is a root of $\rho_L(A)$ because it also lies on a selected line $M\in R$ and both labels equal $f$ there. Since $\deg\rho_L(A)\le D$, more than $D$ such roots force the identity.
AE-16 [lemma/drafting] deps=AE-15 :: Global characteristic-safe cleanup :: Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient gcd in $Z$. Delete content lines and the degeneracies of a formal directional derivative coprime to $B$; this removes at most $pD^2+D|T|$ accepted edges. Then repeatedly delete vertices of current degree at most $D$. The surviving graph $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ satisfies $$e(G_1)\ge a_\theta\epsilon-\left(\frac Dp\right)^2-\frac{3D}{p}\ge\frac{a_\theta\epsilon}{2},\qquad \min_{v\in X_1\sqcup T_1}\deg_{G_1}(v)>D.$$ Moreover, $$B(L(t),P_L(t))=0\quad(L\in T_1),\qquad B(x,f(x))=0,\quad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0\quad(x\in X_1).$$
AE-17 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-16 :: Simple-root factor-pure partition :: Factor the primitive squarefree relation as $B=c\prod_{i=1}^sC_i$ over $\mathbb F_p$. Every $C_i$ depends on $Z$, so $$s\le\frac{\operatorname{wdeg}(B)}d\le\frac Dd\le K_\theta.$$ At every point of $G_1$, exactly one factor vanishes because $B_Z\ne0$. Every line has exactly one identically vanishing factor, and an edge joins a line and point carrying the same factor. Thus $G_1$ is the disjoint union of factor-pure classes, and every vertex retains its full $G_1$-degree inside its class.
AE-18 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-17 :: Constant-factor concentration :: Some factor $C_i$ with $\Delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i)$ has a class $G_i=(X_i,T_i,E_i)$ satisfying $$e(E_i)\ge\frac{e(G_1)}s\ge\frac{a_\theta\epsilon}{2K_\theta},\qquad \min_{v\in X_i\sqcup T_i}\deg_{G_i}(v)>D\ge\Delta_i.$$
AE-19 [obligation/drafting] deps=AE-18 :: Simple-seed factor linearization :: Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible and $Z$-dependent with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(C)\le D<p$. Suppose $C(b,\alpha)=0$, $C_Z(b,\alpha)\ne0$, and more than $\Delta$ distinct affine lines through $b$ carry polynomials $P_L$ of degree at most $d$ such that $$P_L(b)=\alpha,\qquad C(L(t),P_L(t))=0.$$ Then there are $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ and $u\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ such that $$C=u(Z-Q).$$ Applied to AE-18, this linearizes $C_i$.
AE-20 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-19 :: Disconnected factor aggregation :: Let $C_i=u(Z-Q)$ be the factor from AE-19. Every $x\in X_i$ satisfies $Q(x)=f(x)$, including points in different connected components. Since each point lies on at most $p+1$ affine lines, $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{|X_i|}{p^2}\ge\frac{|E_i|}{p^2(p+1)}=e(E_i)\ge\frac{a_\theta\epsilon}{2K_\theta}.$$
AE-21 [theorem/blocked] deps=AE-20 :: Conditional fixed-theta algebraic engine :: Assuming AE-12 and AE-19, for every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$ there are $C_\theta,c_\theta>0$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\theta}$$ implies the existence of one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\Pr[Q=f]\ge c_\theta\epsilon,$$ where one may take $c_\theta=a_\theta/(2K_\theta)$.
AE-22 [theorem/blocked] deps=AE-21 :: Near-linear algebraic propagation engine :: For every fixed $0<\eta<1$, there are constants $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, and every accepted-incidence instance of density $\epsilon$, $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\eta}$$ implies that some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$

ROUND 1 | Round 1 records the complete exact accepted KTZ component chain in `E2E-04` through `E2E-11`; its available fixed-component refinement reaches only the conditional square-root frontier `E2E-12`. The near-linear route replaces cardinality interpolation with the open bounded-weight rank-or-decode interface `E2E-15`. Its relation branch gives a squarefree global relation, reached-root extension, characteristic-safe cleanup, a positive-mass simple-root core, and only $O_\theta(1)$ factor classes. A heavy factor can then be linearized as $Z-Q$, allowing all of its edges across all components to charge directly to one global polynomial without Vinh mixing. The goal remains blocked on the new incidence dichotomy and exact audits of the strengthened algebraic transfers; the uniform exact exponent-one endpoint is separately refuted.

E2E-01 [definition/candidate] deps= :: Bivariate line-versus-point instance :: Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, a function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and, after fixing an affine parametrization of every line $L$, a polynomial $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ with $\deg P_L\le d$. Put $$\rho=d/p,\qquad \epsilon=\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)],$$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\max_{\deg_{\mathrm{tot}}Q\le d}\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)].$$
E2E-02 [definition/candidate] deps=E2E-01 :: Accepted graph and completion predicate :: Let $$G_{\mathrm{acc}}=(\mathbb F_p^2,\mathcal L_p,E_{\mathrm{acc}}),\qquad E_{\mathrm{acc}}=\{(x,L):x\in L,\ P_L(x)=f(x)\}.$$ For $G=(X,T,E)\subseteq G_{\mathrm{acc}}$, set $e(G)=|E|/[p^2(p+1)]$. For $c>0$, let $\mathsf{Done}_c$ assert that some total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $Q$ satisfies $\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c\epsilon$.
E2E-03 [reduction/candidate] deps=E2E-01 :: Fixed-eta reduction :: For fixed $\eta>0$, set $\theta=\min\{\eta,1/4\}$. Then $0<\theta\le1/4$ and, because $0<\rho<1$, $$\rho^{1-\eta}\ge\rho^{1-\theta}.$$

E2E-14 [definition/candidate] deps=E2E-02 :: Rank-reaching relation interface :: For $D\ge d$, put $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\}$$ and define $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\prod_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},

ROUND 2 | Round 2 preserves the exact accepted KTZ component chain `E2E-04`–`E2E-12`, whose available unconditional machinery stops at the square-root frontier. The near-linear spine is `E2E-15`–`E2E-24`: an algebra-aware rank-or-decode dichotomy must retain $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ mass while producing weighted degree $D=O_\theta(d)$. That bound makes the accepted whole-relation cleanup loss $O_\theta(d/p)$ absorbable and leaves only $O_\theta(1)$ irreducible factors. A heavy simple factor then linearizes as $Z-Q$, and all of its edges—across disconnected components—charge to one global polynomial without Vinh mixing. The factor lift is mathematically supported but lacks a fresh exact-accepted standalone source. Accepted round-2 singular-pencil examples show that its simple-root hypothesis cannot be weakened to absolute irreducibility, smooth resultants, or high direction count alone. The goal remains blocked on the low-weight incidence dichotomy and exact audits of the strengthened algebraic transfers; the uniform $Cd/p$ endpoint remains separately refuted.

E2E-10 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-09 :: Accepted KTZ simple roots :: Every point vertex $x$ of $G_1$ satisfies $$B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
E2E-11 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-10 :: Accepted KTZ component decoder :: Under `E2E-04`, there is one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{k}{800p}.$$
E2E-12 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-11 :: Conditional square-root frontier :: Let $k_0=\lfloor\epsilon p/100\rfloor$. If a component of the KTZ-pruned core has line measure $\tau\le\Lambda\epsilon$ and $$\epsilon\ge\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\cdot2^{40}\Lambda}\left(\frac d p\right)^{1/2},$$ then one degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial has agreement at least $\epsilon/160000$.
E2E-13 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-02 :: Universal reaching-cover obstruction :: For $p\ge103$ and $2\le K\le p/(100\log p)$, there is a legal constant-label table with $$\frac1{2K}\le\epsilon\le\frac2K$$ such that every family reaching half of every accepted line has $$|R|\ge\frac{Kp}{16}\ge\frac{p}{32\epsilon},$$ although a constant polynomial already has agreement at least $\epsilon/2$.
E2E-14 [definition/candidate] deps=E2E-02 :: Rank-reaching relation interface :: For $d\le D<p$, put $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\}$$ and $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\bigoplus_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},
\qquad A\mapsto\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\bigr)_{M\in R}.$$ For an accepted $G=(X,T,E)$, define $$\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)=\{x:(x,L)\in E,
\ \exists M\in R\setminus\{L\},\ (x,M)\in E\}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching when $R\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $\operatorname{rank}\Phi_{R,D}<\dim V_D$, and $|\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)|>D$ for every $L\in T$.
E2E-15 [obligation/open] deps=E2E-03,E2E-14 :: Bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy :: For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$, there exist $C_\theta,K_\theta>0$ and $0<a_\theta,c_\theta\le1$ such that, whenever $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\rho^{1-\theta},$$ either $\mathsf{Done}_{c_\theta}$ holds, or there are an accepted graph $G=(X,T,E)$, a set $R\subseteq T$, and an integer $D$ such that $$e(G)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,
\qquad d\le D\le K_\theta d,$$ and $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching.
E2E-16 [reduction/candidate] deps=E2E-15 :: Near-linear parameter gate :: In the relation branch, $$\frac{D}{\epsilon p}\le\frac{K_\theta}{C_\theta}\rho^\theta.$$ After enlarging $C_\theta$ so that $C_\theta\ge10^5K_\theta/a_\theta$, one has $$D\le\frac{a_\theta\epsilon p}{10^5}<p.$$
E2E-17 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-16 :: Squarefree rank-kernel relation :: For the rank-reaching tuple in the relation branch, there is a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ such that $$A_Z\not\equiv0,
\qquad A(M(t),P_M(t))\equiv0\quad(M\in R).$$
E2E-18 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-17 :: Rank-reaching root extension :: For every line vertex $L\in T$, $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$
E2E-19 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-18 :: Whole-relation cleanup at bounded weight :: Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content in $Z$. Then $B$ is primitive in $Z$, squarefree, $Z$-dependent, and of total degree below $p$. There are a formal directional derivative $\mathcal D$ and an exceptional set $E_{\rm exc}\subseteq E$ such that $$\gcd(B,\mathcal DB)=1,
\qquad |E_{\rm exc}|\le pD^2+D|T|.$$ Every line incident to $E\setminus E_{\rm exc}$ satisfies the $B$-identity, and $\mathcal DB$ is nonzero at every edge of $E\setminus E_{\rm exc}$.
E2E-20 [reduction/drafting] deps=E2E-19 :: Global clean simple-root core :: After enlarging $C_\theta$ by a constant depending only on $a_\theta,K_\theta$, deleting $E_{\rm exc}$ and iteratively deleting vertices of current degree at most $D$ leaves an accepted graph $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ satisfying $$e(G_1)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2}\epsilon,
\qquad \operatorname{mindeg}(G_1)>D.$$ Every $L\in T_1$ satisfies $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, and every $x\in X_1$ satisfies $$B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
E2E-21 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-20 :: Bounded factor partition and heavy class :: Factor $$B=c\prod_{i=1}^s C_i$$ into distinct irreducibles and put $\Delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}C_i$. Then every $C_i$ depends on $Z$ and $$s\le\deg_ZB\le\frac D d\le K_\theta.$$ The graph $G_1$ partitions into factor classes $F_i$, each preserving all vertex degrees and satisfying $$C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0,
\qquad C_i(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0.$$ For some $i$, $$e(F_i)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon,
\qquad \operatorname{mindeg}(F_i)>D\ge\Delta_i.$$
E2E-22 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-21 :: High-direction factor linearization :: For the heavy class $F_i$, there exist $u\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ and $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ such that $$C_i=u(Z-Q).$$ Consequently, $$Q(x)=f(x)$$ for every point vertex $x$ of $F_i$.
E2E-23 [reduction/drafting] deps=E2E-15,E2E-22 :: Vinh-free aggregation to one polynomial :: In the relation branch, if $X_i$ and $E_i$ are the point and edge sets of the heavy class, then $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{|X_i|}{p^2}\ge\frac{|E_i|}{p^2(p+1)}=e(F_i)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon.$$ Together with the direct branch of `E2E-15`, this proves $\mathsf{Done}_{c_\theta^*}$ for $$c_\theta^*=\min\left\{c_\theta,\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\right\}.$$
E2E-24 [reduction/blocked] deps=E2E-03,E2E-23 :: Close every fixed exponent :: For every fixed $\eta>0$, there are $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\rho^{1-\eta}$$ implies that some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ satisfies $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
E2E-25 [obligation/refuted] deps=E2E-01 :: Uniform exact-linear endpoint :: There exist absolute constants $C,c>0$ such that every legal instance satisfying $$\epsilon\ge C\frac d p$$ also satisfies $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c\epsilon.$$
E2E-26 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-01 :: Accepted singular-lift obstruction :: For $p>4d+1$ and $D=2d$, there is an absolutely irreducible $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree $D$ supporting more than $D$ distinct degree-at-most-$d$ formal graph lines through one base point, while no $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ satisfies $C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0$. On every supported graph, all $D-d$ vertically ramified lifts are singular.
E2E-GOAL [theorem/blocked] deps=E2E-24 :: Near-linear bivariate soundness :: For every fixed $\eta>0$ there exist $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, and every legal bivariate line table, $$\operatorname{Agr}_{\rm LVP}(f,P)\ge C_\eta\left(\frac d p\right)^{1-\eta}$$ implies that there is one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\operatorname{Agr}_{\rm LVP}(f,P).$$

FILE: research_state/campaign-10-ultra/roadmaps/incidence-engine/round-0001.json
ROUND 1 | The exact corpus-mapped component chain gives a conditional exponent-$1/2$ decoder, while component support and universal cover arguments show that the same cardinality-based route cannot reach exponent $1-o(1)$. The proposed critical path replaces small $|R|$ by low labeled-constraint rank: preserve $\Omega_\eta(\epsilon)$ accepted mass and obtain a relation of weighted degree $D=O_\eta(d)$. Then cleanup costs only $O_\eta(d/p)$, simple roots split the graph among $O_\eta(1)$ factors, and one factor can yield one global polynomial with $\Omega_\eta(\epsilon)$ agreement. The main open incidence node is the low-weight sparsify-or-decode dichotomy; factor linearization also needs a fresh exact proof audit.
CRITICAL: IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH -> IE-L-POPULAR-CORE -> IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY -> IE-L-RANK-INTERP -> IE-L-RANK-EXTEND -> IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT -> IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN -> IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE -> IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT -> IE-R-CLOSE -> IE-GOAL
IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH [definition/candidate] deps= :: Accepted-incidence graph :: Let $\mathcal L_p$ be the $p(p+1)$ affine lines of $\mathbb F_p^2$ and let $$E_{\rm acc}=\{(x,L):x\in L\text{ and }P_L(x)=f(x)\}.$$ Put $\epsilon=|E_{\rm acc}|/[p^2(p+1)]$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\max_{\deg Q\le d}\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)].$$ For $G=(X,T,E)\subseteq E_{\rm acc}$ define $$e(G)=\frac{|E|}{p^2(p+1)},\quad \mu(G)=\frac{|X|}{p^2},\quad \tau(G)=\frac{|T|}{p(p+1)},\quad k(G)=\operatorname{mindeg}(G).$$
IE-D-RANK-REACH [definition/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Rank-reaching line family :: For $d\le D<p$, let $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\},\qquad N_d(D)=\dim V_D,$$ and $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\bigoplus_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\qquad A\mapsto\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\bigr)_{M\in R}.$$ For $G=(X,T,E)$ set $$N_G(L)=\{x:(x,L)\in E\},$$ $$\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)=\{x\in N_G(L):\exists M\in R\setminus\{L\},\ (x,M)\in E\}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching if $R\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $\operatorname{rank}\Phi_{R,D}<N_d(D)$, and $|\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)|>D$ for every $L\in T$.
IE-L-POPULAR-CORE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Popularity pruning :: If an accepted carrier has edge mass $\delta>0$ and line support at most $\sigma$, then for $k_0=\lfloor\delta p/100\rfloor$ it contains a subgraph $G_0$ with $$e(G_0)\ge\delta/4,\qquad k(G_0)\ge k_0,\qquad \delta/4\le\tau(G_0)\le\sigma.$$
IE-L-COMP-MEASURE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Accepted component measures :: Let $G=(X,T,E)$ be connected with minimum degree $k$, line measure $\tau$, and $C_*=2^{40}$. If $$k^2\ge C_*dp,\qquad k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\mu(G)\ge\frac{k}{4p},\qquad \tau\ge\frac{k}{4p}.$$
IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-COMP-MEASURE :: Component-sensitive reaching family :: Under `IE-L-COMP-MEASURE`, writing $t=|T|$, there is $R\subseteq T$ such that $$\frac{4t}{k}\le |R|\le\frac{16t}{k}$$ and $$|\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)|>\frac12\deg_G(L)$$ for every $L\in T$. In particular, $|R|=\Theta(\tau p^2/k)$.
IE-L-STANDARD-EXPLAIN [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE :: Standard explainer and extension :: Let $r=|R|$ and $$D=16\bigl(\lfloor\sqrt{dr}\rfloor+1\bigr).$$ Under the component hypotheses there is a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ with $A_Z\not\equiv0$, $D/k<1/5000$, and $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$$ for every $L\in T$.
IE-L-STANDARD-CLEAN [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-STANDARD-EXPLAIN :: Standard simple-root core :: Write $A=HB$ with $B$ primitive in $Z$. At most $pD^2+D|T|$ accepted edges are exceptional. Deleting them and peeling below $\lfloor k/100\rfloor$ leaves a nonempty graph $G_1$ of minimum degree at least $\lfloor k/100\rfloor$ such that $$B(x,f(x))=0,\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$$ at every point vertex.
IE-T-COMP-DECODE [theorem/candidate] deps=IE-L-STANDARD-CLEAN :: Component-sensitive decoder :: If a connected accepted component of minimum degree $k$ and line measure $\tau$ satisfies $$k^2\ge2^{40}dp,

IE-T-SQRT-MILESTONE [theorem/candidate] deps=IE-L-POPULAR-CORE,IE-T-COMP-DECODE :: Conditional square-root milestone :: Let $G_0$ be a popularity core with minimum degree $k_0=\lfloor\epsilon p/100\rfloor$. If some component has $\tau\le\Lambda\epsilon$ and $$\epsilon\ge\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\cdot2^{40}\Lambda}\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/2},$$ then one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial satisfies $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{\epsilon}{160000}.$$
IE-R-SQRT-CEILING [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-L-COMP-MEASURE :: Support-localization ceiling :: If $c\epsilon p\le k\le C\epsilon p$, $\tau\ge k/(4p)$, and $k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2$, then $$\epsilon^2\ge\frac{C_*}{4C^2}\frac dp.$$
IE-L-COVER-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Universal-cover obstruction :: For $p\ge103$ and $2\le K\le p/(100\log p)$ there is a legal table with $1/(2K)\le\epsilon\le2/K$ such that every $R$ reaching half of every accepted line satisfies $$|R|\ge\frac{Kp}{16}\ge\frac{p}{32\epsilon},$$ while some constant polynomial has agreement at least $\epsilon/2$.
IE-X-EXACT-LINEAR [theorem/refuted] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Exact-linear endpoint :: There are absolute $C,c>0$ such that, for all legal $p,d,f,P$, $$\epsilon\ge C\frac dp\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c\epsilon.$$
IE-L-RANK-BARRIER [literature_input/candidate] deps=IE-D-RANK-REACH :: Rank-blind multiplicity barrier :: In the regime $n>D$, uniform order-$s$ graph multiplicity certified solely by per-line jet codimension and transferred solely by $s$-fold root counting satisfies $$h^2>\frac{dn}{12}.$$
IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY [obligation/open] deps=IE-D-RANK-REACH,IE-L-POPULAR-CORE,IE-T-COMP-DECODE :: Low-weight sparsify-or-decode dichotomy :: For every $\eta>0$ there are $C_\eta,c_\eta,K_\eta>0$ such that, if $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\eta},$$ then either $\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c_\eta\epsilon$, or the popularity core contains an accepted subgraph $G$ for which there are $R$ and $D$ satisfying $$e(G)\ge c_\eta\epsilon,
\qquad d\le D\le K_\eta d,$$ and $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching.
IE-L-RANK-INTERP [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY :: Low-rank interpolation :: If $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching, then there is a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ such that $$A_Z\not\equiv0,
\qquad A(M(t),P_M(t))\equiv0$$ for every $M\in R$.

IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-RANK-EXTEND :: Exceptional-incidence count :: Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content and $B$ is primitive in $Z$. Then $B$ is squarefree of degree below $p$, and there is a formal derivative $\mathcal D$ with $\gcd(B,\mathcal DB)=1$ such that the content, degenerate-line, and derivative-zero exceptional incidences satisfy $$|E_{\rm exc}|\le pD^2+D|T|.$$
IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN [reduction/drafting] deps=IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT :: Near-lossless cleanup at $D=O_\eta(d)$ :: In the structured branch of `IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY`, if $C_\eta$ is sufficiently large relative to $c_\eta,K_\eta$, exceptional deletion and popularity peeling leave a graph $G_1$ and $c'_\eta>0$ such that $$e(G_1)\ge c'_\eta\epsilon,

\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN :: Bounded-factor concentration :: Factor $$B=c\prod_{i=1}^s C_i.$$ Then every $C_i$ depends on $Z$ and $$s\le\deg_ZB\le D/d\le K_\eta.$$ The simple-root graph partitions into factor classes. For some $C_i$ there is an accepted subgraph $F$ with $$e(F)\ge c''_\eta\epsilon,
\qquad k(F)>\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),$$ such that $C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ on every line vertex and $C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0$ on every point vertex.
IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE :: Factor linearization and global aggregation :: Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(C)<p$. If an accepted factor class $F$ has minimum degree greater than $\Delta$, satisfies $C(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ on every line vertex, and has $C_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$ at every point vertex, then there is $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$C\sim Z-Q$$ and $Q(x)=f(x)$ on every point vertex of $F$. Consequently, $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge e(F).$$
IE-R-CLOSE [reduction/blocked] deps=IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT :: Close the sparsify-or-decode branches :: The dichotomy in `IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY` and lemmas `IE-L-RANK-INTERP` through `IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT` imply constants $C'_\eta,c'_\eta>0$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C'_\eta(d/p)^{1-\eta}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c'_\eta\epsilon.$$
IE-GOAL [theorem/blocked] deps=IE-R-CLOSE :: Near-linear bivariate line-versus-point soundness :: For every fixed $\eta>0$ there exist $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, every $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and every degree-at-most-$d$ affine-line table, $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\eta}$$ implies the existence of one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
