ROUND 2 | Round 2 keeps popularity pruning, the accepted component sampler, and its conditional square-root decoder as a baseline. The accepted integral-cover obstruction and the revise-grade fractional-cover and collision identities rule out specific density-only selector or pair-energy shortcuts, not soundness or every graph-based argument. The near-linear spine asks instead for a label-aware rank-or-decode configuration of mass $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ and integer weight $D=O_\theta(d)$, without requiring a whole-line restriction. The parameter gate makes $D/p=O_\theta(d/p)$ a small fraction of $\epsilon$; rank extraction, reached-root extension, primitive vertical cleanup, and global degree-$D$ peeling then preserve constant mass. Simple roots partition the survivor into at most $D/d=O_\theta(1)$ factor classes while preserving degrees. A standalone seed-point lift would force one heavy factor to be $Z-Q$, after which all disconnected components of that factor aggregate to the same global $Q$ without Vinh mixing. The principal research bottlenecks are the bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy and factor linearization; the intervening drafting reductions also require exact audits and do not constitute verified progress.
CRITICAL: IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH -> IE-D-RANK-REACH -> IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY -> IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE -> IE-L-RANK-INTERP -> IE-L-RANK-EXTEND -> IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE -> IE-L-VERTICAL-SEPARABLE -> IE-L-WEIGHTED-RAMIFICATION -> IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT -> IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN -> IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE -> IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT -> IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE -> IE-R-CLOSE -> IE-GOAL
IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH [definition/candidate] deps= :: Accepted-incidence graph :: Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, a function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and the affine-line set $\mathcal L_p$. For each $L\in\mathcal L_p$, fix a bijective affine parametrization $L:\mathbb F_p\to L$ and a polynomial $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$. Define $$E_{\rm acc}=\{(L(t),L):L\in\mathcal L_p,\ t\in\mathbb F_p,\ P_L(t)=f(L(t))\},\qquad \epsilon=\frac{|E_{\rm acc}|}{p^2(p+1)},$$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\max_{\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d}\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)].$$ For $G=(X,T,E)\subseteq E_{\rm acc}$ put $$e(G)=\frac{|E|}{p^2(p+1)},\quad \mu(G)=\frac{|X|}{p^2},\quad \tau(G)=\frac{|T|}{p(p+1)},\quad k(G)=\operatorname{mindeg}(G).$$
IE-D-RANK-REACH [definition/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Rank-reaching structured family :: For an integer $D$ with $d\le D<p$, let $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\},\qquad N_d(D)=\dim V_D,$$ and $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\bigoplus_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\qquad A\mapsto\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\bigr)_{M\in R}.$$ For $G=(X,T,E)$ define $$N_G(L)=\{x:(x,L)\in E\},$$ $$\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)=\{x\in N_G(L):\exists M\in R\setminus\{L\},\ x\in N_G(M)\}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching if $R\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $\operatorname{rank}\Phi_{R,D}<N_d(D)$, and $|\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>D$ for every $L\in T$.
IE-L-POPULAR-CORE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Popularity pruning :: If an accepted carrier $G$ has $e(G)=\delta>0$ and $\tau(G)\le\sigma$, then, for $k_0=\lfloor\delta p/100\rfloor$, it contains $G_0$ with $$e(G_0)\ge\delta/4,\qquad k(G_0)\ge k_0,\qquad \delta/4\le\tau(G_0)\le\sigma.$$
IE-L-COMP-MEASURE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Accepted component concentration :: Let $G$ be a connected accepted graph with minimum degree $k$, line measure $\tau$, and $C_*=2^{40}$. If $$k^2\ge C_*dp,\qquad k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\mu(G)\ge\frac{k}{4p},\qquad \tau\ge\frac{k}{4p}.$$
IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-COMP-MEASURE :: Component-sensitive reaching sample :: Under `IE-L-COMP-MEASURE`, writing $t=|T|$, there exists $R\subseteq T$ such that $$\frac{4t}{k}\le |R|\le\frac{16t}{k}$$ and $$|\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>\frac12\deg_G(L)$$ for every $L\in T$. Hence $|R|=\Theta(\tau p^2/k)$.
IE-T-COMP-DECODE [theorem/candidate] deps=IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE :: Component-sensitive decoder :: If a connected accepted graph of minimum degree $k$ and line measure $\tau$ satisfies $$k^2\ge2^{40}dp,\qquad k^3\ge2^{40}d\tau p^2,$$ then there is one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{k}{800p}.$$
IE-T-SQRT-MILESTONE [theorem/candidate] deps=IE-L-POPULAR-CORE,IE-T-COMP-DECODE :: Conditional square-root milestone :: Let $\Lambda\ge1$, and let $G_0$ be a popularity core with minimum degree $\lfloor\epsilon p/100\rfloor$. If some connected component of $G_0$ has line measure at most $\Lambda\epsilon$ and $$\epsilon\ge\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\cdot2^{40}\Lambda}\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/2},$$ then one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial satisfies $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{\epsilon}{160000}.$$
IE-R-SQRT-CEILING [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-L-COMP-MEASURE :: Support-localization ceiling :: If $$0<k\le C\epsilon p,\qquad \tau\ge k/(4p),\qquad k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\epsilon^2\ge\frac{C_*}{4C^2}\frac dp.$$
IE-L-COVER-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Universal integral-cover obstruction :: For $p\ge103$ and $2\le K\le p/(100\log p)$ there is a legal table with $$\frac1{2K}\le\epsilon\le\frac2K$$ such that every $R$ reaching half of every accepted line satisfies $$|R|\ge\frac{Kp}{16}\ge\frac{p}{32\epsilon},$$ while some constant polynomial has agreement at least $\epsilon/2$.
IE-L-FRACTIONAL-COVER-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Regular fractional-cover obstruction :: There is a legal full-line-support table with $\epsilon=d/p$, every line of accepted degree $d$, every point of accepted degree $d$ or $d+1$, and global decoder $Q=0$. Put $N(L)=\{x:(x,L)\in E_{\rm acc}\}$. For weights $\lambda_M\ge0$, require for every line $L$ that $$\sum_{x\in N(L)}\min\left\{1,\sum_{\substack{M\ne L\\x\in N(M)}}\lambda_M\right\}\ge\frac d2.$$ The minimum total weight $W=\sum_M\lambda_M$ satisfies $$\frac{p(p+1)}{2(d+1)}\le W\le\frac{p(p+1)}{2(d-1)}=\Theta\left(\frac p\epsilon\right).$$
IE-L-COLLISION-IDENTITY [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Exact two-line concentration identity :: Let $a_x=|\{L:(x,L)\in E_{\rm acc}\}|$ and $$\Gamma_2=\frac{1}{p^3(p+1)}\sum_x a_x(a_x-1).$$ Then $$\Gamma_2=\epsilon^2-\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}p+\frac{\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.$$ In particular, $\Gamma_2\ge\epsilon^2/2$ when $\epsilon\ge2/p$. The variance-free formula is attained by accepting $k$ direction classes, where $\epsilon=k/(p+1)$ and $Q=0$.
IE-X-EXACT-LINEAR [theorem/refuted] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Exact-linear endpoint :: The assertion that there exist absolute $C,c>0$ such that every legal instance satisfies $$\epsilon\ge C\frac dp\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c\epsilon$$ is false, already for $d=101$.
IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY [obligation/open] deps=IE-D-RANK-REACH :: Bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy :: For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$ there exist $C_\theta\ge1$, $0<a_\theta\le1$, and $K_\theta\ge1$ such that, if $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\theta},$$ then either $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,$$ or there exist an accepted subgraph $G=(X,T,E)$, a set $R\subseteq T$, and an integer $D$ such that $$e(G)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,\qquad d\le D\le K_\theta d,$$ and $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching.
IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY :: Near-lossless parameter gate :: After enlarging $C_\theta$ so that $C_\theta\ge16K_\theta/a_\theta$, the structured branch satisfies $$\frac{D}{\epsilon p}\le\frac{K_\theta}{C_\theta}\le\frac{a_\theta}{16},\qquad D\le\frac{a_\theta\epsilon p}{16}<p.$$ Indeed, for $\rho=d/p<1$, one has $\rho=\rho^{1-\theta}\rho^\theta\le\epsilon/C_\theta$.
IE-L-RANK-INTERP [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE :: Low-rank squarefree interpolation :: In the structured branch there exists a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ such that $$A_Z\not\equiv0,
\qquad A(M(t),P_M(t))\equiv0$$ for every $M\in R$.
IE-L-RANK-EXTEND [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-RANK-INTERP :: Reached-root extension :: For every line vertex $L$ of $G$, the more than $D$ distinct reached accepted points force $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$
IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-L-RANK-EXTEND :: Primitive content reduction :: Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content, $B$ is primitive in $Z$, $h=\deg H$, and $\delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(B)$. Then $B$ is squarefree and $Z$-dependent, $$d\le\delta,\qquad h+\delta\le D.$$ At most $h$ affine lines satisfy $H(L(t))\equiv0$; every other line vertex of $G$ satisfies $$B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$
IE-L-VERTICAL-SEPARABLE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE :: Prime-field vertical separability :: Under `IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE`, $$\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$
IE-L-WEIGHTED-RAMIFICATION [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-VERTICAL-SEPARABLE :: Weighted ramification-line bound :: Let $r=\deg_ZB$. The number of degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines satisfying both $$B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0,\qquad B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$$ is at most $$(2r-1)\delta-dr^2\le\delta^2/d-\delta.$$
IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-L-WEIGHTED-RAMIFICATION :: Prime-field vertical cleanup count :: At most $$h+\delta^2/d-\delta\le D^2/d-D$$ lines are content-trivial or identically $B_Z$-ramified. On every other line, $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, while the nonzero polynomial $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))$ has at most $\delta-d\le D-d$ roots. Thus deleting all nonsimple accepted incidences costs at most $$(D^2/d-D)/(p(p+1))+(D-d)/p\le D^2/p^2+D/p.$$
IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN [reduction/drafting] deps=IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT :: Near-lossless global cleanup :: After the vertical deletion, iteratively delete every point or line vertex of current degree at most $D$. Peeling costs less than $$\frac{D(|\mathbb F_p^2|+|\mathcal L_p|)}{p^2(p+1)}<\frac{2D}{p}.$$ There remains a nonempty graph $G_1$ satisfying $$e(G_1)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2}\epsilon,\qquad k(G_1)>D,$$ with $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ on every line vertex and $$B(x,f(x))=0,\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$$ at every point vertex.
IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN :: Simple-root factor partition :: Factor $$B=c\prod_{i=1}^s C_i$$ into distinct irreducibles and put $\Delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i)$. Primitivity makes every $C_i$ depend on $Z$, so $$s\le\deg_ZB\le D/d\le K_\theta.$$ At a simple lifted point exactly one factor vanishes; every incident line has that same factor identity. Hence the simple-root graph is a disjoint union of factor classes $F_i$, each preserving every vertex degree. For some $i$, $$e(F_i)\ge\frac{e(G_1)}s\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon,\qquad k(F_i)>D\ge\Delta_i,$$ and its vertices satisfy $$C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$$ on every line and $$C_i(x,f(x))=0,\qquad C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0$$ at every point.
IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT [obligation/drafting] deps=IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE :: Seed-point factor linearization :: Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(C)\le D<p$. If a nonempty accepted factor class $F$ has $k(F)>D$, satisfies $C(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ on every line vertex, and has $C_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$ at every point vertex, then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$C\sim Z-Q.$$
IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT :: Global factor aggregation :: For the factor class $F$ from `IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE`, the identities $C(x,f(x))=0$ and $C\sim Z-Q$ imply $Q(x)=f(x)$ at every point vertex of $F$. Since every affine point lies on at most $p+1$ lines, $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{|X(F)|}{p^2}\ge e(F)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon.$$
IE-R-CLOSE [reduction/blocked] deps=IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE :: Close the direct and structured branches :: For arbitrary fixed $\eta>0$, set $\theta=\min\{\eta,1/4\}$. Since $0<d/p<1$ and $$(d/p)^{1-\eta}\ge(d/p)^{1-\theta},$$ the dichotomy and factor aggregation yield constants $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta(d/p)^{1-\eta}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
IE-GOAL [theorem/blocked] deps=IE-R-CLOSE :: Near-linear bivariate line-versus-point soundness :: For every fixed $\eta>0$ there exist $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, integer $100<d<p$, function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and degree-at-most-$d$ affine-line table, $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\eta}$$ implies the existence of one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$

RESEARCHER-0009 LEMMA BOOK
ID: researcher-0009:P1.1 | STEP: P1 | STATUS: proved | Distinct-star counts
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ have agreement $\epsilon$, accepted point degrees $a_x$, and accepted line degrees $b_L$. For integers $u,s\ge0$, write $(u)_0=1$ and, for $s\ge1$, $(u)_s=u(u-1)\cdots(u-s+1)$. For $0\le s\le p+1$, let $\Gamma_s^P$ be the probability that, after choosing a uniform point and an ordered $s$-tuple of distinct incident lines uniformly, all sampled incidences are accepted. For $0\le s\le p$, define $\Gamma_s^L$ analogously using a uniform line and an ordered $s$-tuple of distinct points on it. Then

$$\Gamma_s^P=\frac{\mathbb E_x[(a_x)_s]}{(p+1)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p+1),$$

and

$$\Gamma_s^L=\frac{\mathbb E_L[(b_L)_s]}{(p)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p).$$

ID: researcher-0009:P2.1 | STEP: P2 | STATUS: proved | Degree-moment envelopes
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ have agreement $\epsilon$. For every real $\mu\ge0$, put $k=\lfloor\mu\rfloor$ and $\rho=\mu-k$, and define

$$J_s(\mu)=(1-\rho)(k)_s+\rho(k+1)_s.$$

For every integer $r\ge1$, let $\widetilde\Gamma_r^P$ and $\widetilde\Gamma_r^L$ be the analogous all-accepted probabilities when the incident lines or points are sampled independently with replacement. Then

$$\Gamma_s^P\ge\frac{J_s((p+1)\epsilon)}{(p+1)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p+1),$$

$$\Gamma_s^L\ge\frac{J_s(p\epsilon)}{(p)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p),$$

and

$$\widetilde\Gamma_r^P\ge\epsilon^r,\qquad \widetilde\Gamma_r^L\ge\epsilon^r\qquad(r\ge1).$$

ID: researcher-0009:P3.1 | STEP: P3 | STATUS: proved | Affine carrier
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Define

$$S_d=\{0,1,\ldots,d-1\}\subset\mathbb F_p,\qquad f(x,y)=0.$$

For every $m,b\in\mathbb F_p$, parameterize the nonvertical line $L_{m,b}$ by $L_{m,b}(t)=(t,mt+b)$ and assign it the label

$$P_{m,b}(t)=\prod_{s\in S_d}(t+m-s).$$

For every $c\in\mathbb F_p$, parameterize the vertical line $V_c$ by $V_c(t)=(c,t)$ and assign it the label

$$P_{V_c}(t)=\prod_{s\in S_d}(t-s).$$

These data form a valid affine line-versus-point table of agreement $\epsilon_0=d/p$. Every label has formal degree exactly $d$, every affine line has accepted degree $d$, exactly $pd$ points have accepted degree $d+1$, and the remaining $p(p-d)$ points have accepted degree $d$.

ID: researcher-0009:P4.1 | STEP: P4 | STATUS: proved | Carrier graph
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let the valid table be the table defined in P3. Its accepted bipartite point-line graph is connected, and every point of $\mathbb F_p^2$ and every affine line is a nonisolated vertex.

ID: researcher-0009:P5.1 | STEP: P5 | STATUS: proved | Carrier moments
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let the table defined in P3 have agreement $\epsilon_0=d/p$. Then

$$\Gamma_s^P=\frac{(1-d/p)(d)_s+(d/p)(d+1)_s}{(p+1)_s}=\frac{J_s((p+1)\epsilon_0)}{(p+1)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p+1),$$

$$\Gamma_s^L=\frac{(d)_s}{(p)_s}=\frac{J_s(p\epsilon_0)}{(p)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p),$$

and, for every integer $r\ge1$,

$$\widetilde\Gamma_r^L=\epsilon_0^r.$$

ID: researcher-0009:P6.1 | STEP: P6 | STATUS: proved | Fractional reaching
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let the table defined in P3 have accepted edge set $E$ and agreement $\epsilon_0=d/p$. For nonnegative real line weights $\lambda=(\lambda_M)$, define

$$u_{x,L}(\lambda)=\min\left\{1,\sum_{\substack{M\ni x,\ M\ne L\\(x,M)\in E}}\lambda_M\right\}.$$

For $0<\theta\le1$, call $\lambda$ $\theta$-reaching if

$$\sum_{x:(x,L)\in E}u_{x,L}(\lambda)\ge\theta d$$

for every affine line $L$, and let $\operatorname{OPT}_\theta$ be the minimum of $\sum_M\lambda_M$ over all such weights. Then

$$\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d+1}\le\operatorname{OPT}_\theta\le\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d-1}.$$

ID: researcher-0009:P7.1 | STEP: P7 | STATUS: proved | Affine incidence neighborhoods
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $G$ be the bipartite accepted-incidence graph of a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$. Then $G$ contains no $K_{2,2}$, and every set of at least two distinct vertices on either shore of $G$ has at most one common neighbor.

ID: researcher-0009:P8.1 | STEP: P8 | STATUS: proved | Compatibility graph degrees
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ have local agreement $\epsilon$. For each point $x$, let $a_x$ be its accepted-line degree, put $\mu=(p+1)\epsilon$ and $N=p(p+1)$, and let $H$ be the graph on the $N$ affine lines in which two distinct nonparallel lines are adjacent exactly when both accept their unique intersection. If $\bar\Delta=2e(H)/N$, then

$$e(H)=\sum_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}\binom{a_x}{2}$$

and

$$\bar\Delta\ge\frac{p\mu(\mu-1)}{p+1}.$$

ID: researcher-0009:P9.1 | STEP: P9 | STATUS: proved | Auxiliary neighborhood moments
Under the hypotheses and notation of P8, let $t$ be an integer with $1\le t\le\lfloor\bar\Delta\rfloor$, and define

$$M_t(H)=\frac{\sum_{L\in V(H)}\binom{\deg_H(L)}t}{\binom Nt}.$$

There is a set $S\subseteq V(H)$ of $t$ distinct lines such that

$$\left|\bigcap_{M\in S}N_H(M)\right|\ge M_t(H)\ge N\frac{\binom{\lfloor\bar\Delta\rfloor}t}{\binom Nt}.$$

If additionally $\mu\ge2$ and $\bar\Delta\ge2t+2$, then

$$M_t(H)\ge N\left(\frac{\epsilon^2}{4}\right)^t.$$

ID: researcher-0009:P10.1 | STEP: P10 | STATUS: proved | Seed-intersection concurrency
Under the hypotheses and notation of P9, fix one $t$-line seed set $S$ supplied there, and write

$$N_H(S)=\bigcap_{M\in S}N_H(M).$$

For $L\in N_H(S)$, define

$$h_L=\left|\{L\cap M:M\in S\}\right|.$$

For this fixed $S$, every integer $h$ with $1\le h\le t$ satisfies

$$\left|\{L\in N_H(S):h_L\ge h\}\right|\ge M_t(H)-\frac{\binom t2(p+1)}{t-h+1}.$$

ID: researcher-0009:P11.1 | STEP: P11 | STATUS: proved | DRC parameter comparison
Fix an integer $d\ge101$, an integer $t\ge1$, and a constant $C>0$. Put

$$D_t=16(\lfloor\sqrt{dt}\rfloor+1).$$

Let $h_L$ be the number of distinct seed intersections on a common-neighbor target line for a $t$-line seed set, as in P10. If transfer requires $h_L>D_t$, then necessarily $t>256d$. For primes $p>d$, put

$$\epsilon_p=C\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3},\qquad N_p=p(p+1).$$

For every fixed $d$, $t>256d$, and $C$,

$$N_p\left(\frac{\epsilon_p^2}{4}\right)^t=O_{C,d,t}\left(p^{2-2t/3}\right)\longrightarrow0$$

as $p\to\infty$ through primes.

ID: researcher-0009:P12.1 | STEP: P12 | STATUS: proved | Carrier scope
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and set $\epsilon_0=d/p$. There exists a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ whose accepted carrier is connected and has positive degree at every affine point and line, which attains both distinct-star moment lower envelopes for every point-centered order $0\le s\le p+1$ and every line-centered order $0\le s\le p$, and whose line-centered with-replacement moment is $\epsilon_0^r$ for every integer $r\ge1$. For every $0<\theta\le1$, the same table satisfies

$$\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d+1}\le\operatorname{OPT}_\theta\le\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d-1}.$$

For this table, the polynomial $Q=0$ has total degree $0\le d$ and agrees with the point table at every point.

ID: researcher-0009:P13.1 | STEP: P13 | STATUS: proved | KTZ parameter chain
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ have point function $f$ and agreement $0<\epsilon\le1$. Suppose the KTZ selector has size $|R|=O(p/\epsilon)$, weighted interpolation uses degree $D=O(\sqrt{d|R|})$, and transfer and lifting require the resulting interpolation scale to fit the budget $D=O(\epsilon p)$. Then the interpolation scale is $D=O(\sqrt{dp/\epsilon})$, and compatibility of these scales requires

$$\epsilon^3=\Omega\left(\frac dp\right).$$

If additionally

$$\epsilon\ge C_{\mathrm{KTZ}}\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3}$$

for the sufficiently large absolute constant in KTZ Theorem 1.1, then there is a polynomial $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ and an absolute constant $c>0$ such that

$$\frac{|\{x\in\mathbb F_p^2:Q(x)=f(x)\}|}{p^2}\ge c\epsilon.$$

RESEARCHER-0010 LEMMA BOOK
ID: researcher-0010:[P1].1 | STEP: [P1] | STATUS: proved | Asymmetric Mixing Bounds
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let $G=(X,T,E)$ be a nonempty accepted point-line subgraph over $\mathbb F_p^2$. Suppose every point of $X$ has degree at least $u$, every line of $T$ has degree at least $v$, $t=|T|$, $\tau=t/[p(p+1)]$, and $w=\min\{u,v\}$. If

$$
uv\ge2^{60}dp
\quad\text{and}\quad
uw^2\ge2^{60}d\tau p^2,
$$

then

$$
\tau\ge\frac{u}{4(p+1)},\qquad
\frac{|X|}{p^2}\ge\frac{v}{4p},\qquad
w^2\ge2^{57}dp.
$$

ID: researcher-0010:[P2].1 | STEP: [P2] | STATUS: proved | Line Selector
Under the hypotheses of the asymmetric mixing bounds, there exists $R\subseteq T$ such that

$$
\frac{4t}{u}\le |R|\le\frac{16t}{u}
\quad\text{and}\quad
|R|\ge p,
$$

and, for every $L\in T$, more than half of the points $x\in N_G(L)$ are incident to a line $M\in R\setminus\{L\}$ with $(x,M)\in E$.

ID: researcher-0010:[P3].1 | STEP: [P3] | STATUS: proved | Squarefree Explainer
Let $R$ be a selector from the preceding entry, put $r=|R|$, and define

$$
D=16\bigl(\lfloor\sqrt{dr}\rfloor+1\bigr).
$$

There exists a nonzero squarefree, $Z$-dependent polynomial $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree at most $D$ such that

$$
A_Z\not\equiv0,
\qquad
A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
$$

for every $L\in R$, and

$$
D<\frac{\min\{u,v\}}{5000}<p.
$$

ID: researcher-0010:[P4].1 | STEP: [P4] | STATUS: proved | Identity Extension
Let $A$ and $D$ be as in the squarefree explainer entry, and let $R$ satisfy the selector property. Then

$$
A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
$$

for every $L\in T$.

ID: researcher-0010:[P5].1 | STEP: [P5] | STATUS: proved | Simple-Root Cleanup
Let $A$ be the squarefree explainer whose identity holds on every line of $T$, and write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the greatest common divisor of the $Z$-coefficients of $A$. Then $B$ is squarefree, $Z$-primitive, $Z$-dependent, has weighted degree at most $D<p$, and satisfies

$$
\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.
$$

After deleting at most

$$
pD^2+Dt
$$

accepted edges, every retained edge $(x,L)$ satisfies

$$
B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0,
\qquad
B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad
B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.
$$

ID: researcher-0010:[P6].1 | STEP: [P6] | STATUS: proved | Asymmetric Pruning
Under the hypotheses of the asymmetric mixing bounds, remove the exceptional edges from the simple-root cleanup and set

$$
u_1=\left\lfloor\frac{u}{100}\right\rfloor,
\qquad
v_1=\left\lfloor\frac{v}{100}\right\rfloor.
$$

Iteratively deleting points of current degree below $u_1$ and lines of current degree below $v_1$ leaves a nonempty subgraph in which every retained edge is a simple root of $B$, the point minimum degree is at least $u_1$, and the line minimum degree is at least $v_1$. Moreover,

$$
u_1\ge\frac{u}{200},
\qquad
v_1\ge\frac{v}{200},
\qquad
u_1,v_1>D.
$$

ID: researcher-0010:[P7].1 | STEP: [P7] | STATUS: proved | Component Recovery
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let $G=(X,T,E)$ be a nonempty accepted subgraph with point minimum degree $u$, line minimum degree $v$, line measure $\tau=|T|/[p(p+1)]$, and $w=\min\{u,v\}$. If

$$
uv\ge2^{60}dp
\quad\text{and}\quad
uw^2\ge2^{60}d\tau p^2,
$$

then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{v}{800p}.
$$

ID: researcher-0010:[P8].1 | STEP: [P8] | STATUS: conditional | Popularity-Tail Translation
Let the full accepted graph have normalized edge mass $\epsilon>0$, let $a_x$ be the accepted degree of $x$, and define

$$
\delta_j=\frac{1}{p^2(p+1)}\sum_{x:a_x\ge j}a_x,
\qquad
u_*=\max\{j\in\{1,\ldots,p+1\}:\delta_j\ge\epsilon/2\}.
$$

If

$$
u_*\epsilon^2\ge2^{70}d,
$$

then there exists a polynomial $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying

$$
\operatorname{agr}(f,Q)\ge\frac{\epsilon}{12800}.
$$

If additionally $0\le a\le1$, $0<\kappa\le1$, and $u_*\ge\kappa\epsilon^ap$, the same conclusion holds whenever

$$
\epsilon\ge
\left(\frac{2^{70}}{\kappa}\right)^{1/(a+2)}
\left(\frac{d}{p}\right)^{1/(a+2)}.
$$

ID: researcher-0010:[P9].1 | STEP: [P9] | STATUS: proved | Template Popularity
Let $T_1,\ldots,T_R$ be disjoint affine-line families, and let $Q_1,\ldots,Q_R\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ have total degree at most $d$. Suppose

$$
P_L=Q_j|_L
$$

for every $L\in T_j$. Define

$$
\tau_j=\frac{|T_j|}{p(p+1)},
\qquad
\tau=\sum_{j=1}^R\tau_j,
\qquad
\alpha=\max_{1\le j\le R}\operatorname{agr}(f,Q_j),
$$

and let $\delta>0$ be the normalized accepted-incidence mass carried by $\bigcup_jT_j$. Then

$$
\delta\le\alpha\tau+
\sqrt{\frac{R\alpha\tau}{p+1}}.
$$

If $R\le\delta(p+1)$, then $\alpha\ge\delta/4$.

ID: researcher-0010:[P10].1 | STEP: [P10] | STATUS: proved | Constant-Label Decoding
Let $T$ be a family of constant-labelled affine lines, let

$$
\tau=\frac{|T|}{p(p+1)},
$$

let $\delta>0$ be its normalized accepted-incidence mass, and put

$$
\mu=\max_{a\in\mathbb F_p}\Pr_x[f(x)=a].
$$

Then

$$
\delta\le\mu\tau+
\sqrt{\frac{\tau}{p+1}}.
$$

If $\delta\ge2\sqrt{\tau/(p+1)}$, then

$$
\mu\ge\frac{\delta}{2\tau}\ge\frac{\delta}{2}.
$$

If these lines carry at least a $\theta$ fraction of total agreement and

$$
\epsilon\ge\frac{2}{\theta\sqrt{p+1}},
$$

then some constant polynomial has agreement at least $\theta\epsilon/2$.

ID: researcher-0010:[P11].1 | STEP: [P11] | STATUS: proved | Star-Moment Identities
Let $a_x$ be the accepted degree of $x$ in a deterministic table over $\mathbb F_p^2$. For $2\le s\le p+1$, let $\Gamma_s$ be the probability that $s$ ordered distinct uniformly sampled lines through a uniformly sampled point all accept there, and write

$$
(n)_s=n(n-1)\cdots(n-s+1).
$$

Then

$$
\Gamma_s=\frac{1}{p^2(p+1)_s}\sum_x(a_x)_s.
$$

If

$$
\mu=\epsilon(p+1)=k+\theta,
\qquad
k=\lfloor\mu\rfloor,
\qquad
0\le\theta<1,
$$

then

$$
\Gamma_s\ge
\frac{(1-\theta)(k)_s+\theta(k+1)_s}{(p+1)_s}.
$$

For $s=2$,

$$
\Gamma_2=\epsilon^2-
\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{p}+
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.
$$

ID: researcher-0010:[P12].1 | STEP: [P12] | STATUS: proved | Modal Occupancy Concentration
Let $p$ be prime and set

$$
r=\left\lfloor\frac{\log p}{4\log\log p}\right\rfloor,
\qquad
u_0=\left\lceil\frac{4\log p}{\log\log p}\right\rceil.
$$

Assume

$$
r\ge133,
\qquad
r^2\le p,
\qquad
p\ge8r!,
\qquad
u_0+1\le17r,
\qquad
\frac{2p}{(u_0+1)!}\le1.
$$

Choose the values $f(x)$ independently and uniformly from $\mathbb F_p$. On each affine line, choose a modal value uniformly using an independent tie-breaking coin and use it as the constant line polynomial. If $M$ is the maximum occupancy of $p$ independent uniform samples from $\mathbb F_p$, $\epsilon$ is the realized local agreement, and

$$
q=\frac{\mathbb E[M]}p=\mathbb E[\epsilon],
$$

then

$$
\frac{r}{65p}\le q\le\frac{17r}{p}
$$

and

$$
\Pr\left[\frac q2\le\epsilon\le\frac{3q}{2}\right]\ge\frac34.
$$

ID: researcher-0010:[P13].1 | STEP: [P13] | STATUS: proved | Modal Table Realization
Fix $S\ge2$. Let $p$ be prime and set

$$
r=\left\lfloor\frac{\log p}{4\log\log p}\right\rfloor,
\qquad
u_0=\left\lceil\frac{4\log p}{\log\log p}\right\rceil,
\qquad
v_0=\left\lceil\frac{8\log p}{\log\log p}\right\rceil.
$$

Assume

$$
r\ge133,
\quad r^2\le p,
\quad p\ge8r!,
\quad u_0+1\le17r,
\quad v_0\le33r,
$$

$$
\frac{2p}{(u_0+1)!}\le1,
\quad
\frac{2p^3}{v_0!}\le\frac18,
\quad
p^{5253}(e/4)^p\le\frac18,
\quad
S\le p+1.
$$

At ambient degree $d=101$, there exists a deterministic table with constant line polynomials such that

$$
\frac{r}{130p}\le\epsilon\le\frac{26r}{p},
$$

$$
\max_{\substack{Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]\\ \deg Q\le101}}
\operatorname{agr}(f,Q)<\frac2p,
$$

and, for every $2\le s\le S$,

$$
\Gamma_s\le2^{s+2}(S-1)\epsilon^s.
$$

Every affine line has at least one and fewer than $v_0$ accepted points.

ID: researcher-0010:[P14].1 | STEP: [P14] | STATUS: refuted | Scale-Free Star-Energy Implication
Fix an integer $s\ge2$, a real number $\gamma>0$, and constants $c_0,c_1>0$. Let a deterministic line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ have ambient degree at most $101$ and local agreement $\epsilon>0$. If

$$
\max_{\substack{Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]\\ \deg Q\le101}}
\operatorname{agr}(f,Q)
\le c_0\epsilon,
$$

then

$$
\Gamma_s\ge c_1\epsilon^{s-\gamma}.
$$

Strict acceptance fails because the exact statement leaves key context and A unquantified, P8 omits the algebra completing both mixing contradictions, the propagation citation omits KTZ Lemma 6.3, and P18 overstates the universal necessity of deletion. All defects are repairable without weakening the mathematical claim.

RESEARCHER-0003 AUDIT
VERDICT: reject
SUMMARY: Reject the exact SHA-identified claim. Its weighted-resultant, vertical-separability, and cleanup arguments are correct under the fuller hypotheses in note Theorem 2, and the KTZ application remains at the benchmark exponent 1/3 with a valid route to one global polynomial. But the exact hashed theorem omits the hypotheses forcing Z-dependence and is concretely false at p=103,d=D=101. Because d/p<1, no exponent greater than 1/3 is obtained. P9 also needs a fixed-density quantifier, and P8 should spell out the adapted pruning step.
FATAL: The exact SHA-identified theorem is false because it omits the hypotheses forcing a Z-dependent interpolant. Let p=103 and d=D=101. Choose 101 horizontal lines Y=a, label one by t^101 and the others by 0. Every weighted-degree-at-most-101 interpolant has form lambda Z+b(X,Y). Comparing the t^101 coefficients on differently labelled lines forces lambda=0. Minimum interpolants are therefore scalar multiples of the squarefree polynomial H(Y)=prod_a(Y-a), of exact weighted degree D. Removing its Z-content gives B=1. All 101 graph lines are H-trivial, whereas the hashed claim bounds their number by floor(D^2/d-D)=0 and its cleanup loss by 0.

ID: researcher-0002:P1.1 | STEP: P1 | STATUS: proved | Component Measures
Let $p$ be prime and let $d$ be an integer satisfying $100<d<p$. Let $f:\mathbb{F}_p^2\to\mathbb{F}_p$, and assign to every affine line $L\subseteq\mathbb{F}_p^2$ a polynomial $P_L$ of degree at most $d$. Let $G=(X,T,E)$ be a connected subgraph of the accepted-incidence graph with minimum degree $k$, where an incidence $(x,L)$ is accepted when $P_L(x)=f(x)$. Put

$$
t=|T|,\qquad \mu(X)=\frac{|X|}{p^2},\qquad \tau=\frac{t}{p(p+1)},\qquad C_*=2^{40}.
$$

Assume

$$
k^2\ge C_*dp,\qquad k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2.
$$

Then

$$
\mu(X)\ge\frac{k}{4p},\qquad \tau\ge\frac{k}{4p}.
$$

In particular, $t\ge k(p+1)/4$.

ID: researcher-0002:P2.1 | STEP: P2 | STATUS: proved | Reaching Family
Under the hypotheses and notation of P1, there exists a set $R\subseteq T$ such that

$$
\frac{4t}{k}\le |R|\le\frac{16t}{k},
$$

and, for every $L\in T$,

$$
\left|\left\{x\in N_G(L):\exists\,M\in R\setminus\{L\}\text{ with }(x,M)\in E\right\}\right|
>\frac12\deg_G(L).
$$

Moreover, $|R|\ge p+1$ and $|R|=\Theta(\tau p^2/k)$ with absolute implied constants.

ID: researcher-0002:P3.1 | STEP: P3 | STATUS: proved | Trivariate Explainer
Under the hypotheses and notation of P1 and P2, put $r=|R|$ and

$$
D=16\left(\left\lfloor\sqrt{dr}\right\rfloor+1\right).
$$

There exists a nonzero squarefree polynomial $A\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree at most $D$ for weights $(1,1,d)$ such that

$$
A_Z\not\equiv0
$$

and

$$
A(M(s),P_M(s))\equiv0\qquad\text{for every }M\in R,
$$

where $M(s)$ is an affine parametrization of $M$. Moreover,

$$
D^2>256dr,
\qquad
D^2\le1024dr\le\frac{16384dt}{k}\le\frac{32768d\tau p^2}{k},
\qquad
\frac{D}{k}<\frac1{5000},
$$

so $D<k\le p<r$ and $D=O\!\left(\sqrt{d\tau p^2/k}\right)$.

ID: researcher-0002:P4.1 | STEP: P4 | STATUS: proved | Linewise Extension
Let $G=(X,T,E)$, $R$, $A$, and $D$ satisfy the hypotheses and conclusions of P2 and P3. Then, for every $L\in T$ and every affine parametrization $L(s)$,

$$
A(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0.
$$

ID: researcher-0002:P5.1 | STEP: P5 | STATUS: proved | Exceptional-Root Cleanup
Under the hypotheses and notation of P4, write

$$
A=\sum_j a_j(X,Y)Z^j,
\qquad
H(X,Y)=\gcd_j a_j(X,Y),
\qquad
B=A/H.
$$

Then $B$ is squarefree, has total degree less than $p$, and satisfies

$$
B(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0
$$

for every $L\in T$ such that $H(L(s))\not\equiv0$. There exists a formal directional derivative $\mathcal{D}$ such that

$$
\gcd(B,\mathcal{D}B)=1.
$$

Define

$$
T_{\mathrm{triv}}=\{L\in T:H(L(s))\equiv0\}
$$

and

$$
T_{\mathrm{deg}}=
\left\{L\in T\setminus T_{\mathrm{triv}}:(\mathcal{D}B)(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0\right\}.
$$

Then

$$
|T_{\mathrm{triv}}|\le D,
\qquad
|T_{\mathrm{deg}}|\le D(D-1),
$$

and every $L\in T\setminus(T_{\mathrm{triv}}\cup T_{\mathrm{deg}})$ satisfies

$$
\left|\{x\in N_G(L):(\mathcal{D}B)(x,f(x))=0\}\right|\le D.
$$

Consequently, for

$$
E_{\mathrm{exc}}=
\left\{(x,L)\in E:L\in T_{\mathrm{triv}}\cup T_{\mathrm{deg}}
\text{ or }(\mathcal{D}B)(x,f(x))=0\right\},
$$

one has

$$
|E_{\mathrm{exc}}|\le pD^2+Dt.
$$

ID: researcher-0002:P6.1 | STEP: P6 | STATUS: proved | Clean Core
Under the hypotheses and notation of P5,

$$
\frac{|E_{\mathrm{exc}}|}{|E|}
\le\frac{D}{k}+\frac{16384dp}{k^2}
<\frac1{4000}.
$$

Set

$$
k_1=\left\lfloor\frac{k}{100}\right\rfloor.
$$

Starting from $(X,T,E\setminus E_{\mathrm{exc}})$ and iteratively deleting every vertex whose current degree is less than $k_1$ removes fewer than

$$
k_1(|X|+t)
\le2\frac{k_1}{k}|E|
\le\frac{|E|}{50}
$$

additional edges and leaves a nonempty subgraph $G_1$ of minimum degree at least $k_1$.

ID: researcher-0002:P7.1 | STEP: P7 | STATUS: proved | Simple Roots
Let $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ be the surviving graph from P6, and let $B$ and $\mathcal{D}$ be as in P5. Then every $x\in X_1$ satisfies

$$
B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad
B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.
$$

ID: researcher-0002:P8.1 | STEP: P8 | STATUS: proved | Polynomial Decoder
Under the hypotheses and notation of P1–P7, there exists a polynomial $Q\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{k}{800p}.
$$

ID: researcher-0002:P9.1 | STEP: P9 | STATUS: conditional | Small-Component Corollary
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let

$$
\epsilon=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)],
$$

where $L$ is uniform among all affine lines of $\mathbb{F}_p^2$ and $x$ is uniform on $L$. Let $G_0$ be a graph supplied by KTZ Lemma 3.1 with minimum degree at least

$$
k_0=\left\lfloor\frac{\epsilon p}{100}\right\rfloor.
$$

Let $\Lambda\ge1$, and suppose $G_0$ has a connected component of line measure $\tau\le\Lambda\epsilon$. Put $C_*=2^{40}$ and assume

$$
\epsilon\ge\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000C_*\Lambda}\left(\frac{d}{p}\right)^{1/2}.
$$

Then there exists $Q\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{\epsilon}{160000}.
$$

ID: researcher-0002:P10.1 | STEP: P10 | STATUS: proved | Balanced-Color Realization
Let $p\ge103$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let $K$ be an integer satisfying

$$
2\le K\le\frac{p}{100\log p}.
$$

There exist a function $f:\mathbb{F}_p^2\to\mathbb{F}_p$ and a constant polynomial $P_L$ on every affine line $L$ such that, with

$$
\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(L)=|\{x\in L:P_L(x)=f(x)\}|
$$

and

$$
\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(x)=|\{L\ni x:P_L(x)=f(x)\}|,
$$

one has, for every affine line $L$ and every point $x$,

$$
\frac{p}{2K}\le\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(L)\le\frac{2p}{K},
$$

$$
\frac{p+1}{2K}\le\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(x)\le\frac{2(p+1)}{K}.
$$

The test agreement satisfies

$$
\frac1{2K}\le\epsilon\le\frac2K.
$$

ID: researcher-0002:P11.1 | STEP: P11 | STATUS: proved | Covering Lower Bound
Let $f$ and the line table be as in P10. For a set $R$ of affine lines, define

$$
S_R=\{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2:\exists\,M\in R\text{ with }x\in M\text{ and }P_M(x)=f(x)\}.
$$

Suppose that every affine line $L$ satisfies

$$
\left|S_R\cap\{x\in L:P_L(x)=f(x)\}\right|
\ge\frac12\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(L).
$$

Then

$$
|R|\ge\frac{Kp}{16}\ge\frac{p}{32\epsilon}.
$$

Moreover, there exists a constant polynomial $Q\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y]$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac1K\ge\frac{\epsilon}{2}.
$$

end-to-end-soundness round 3
SUMMARY: Round 3 retains the independently accepted KTZ component chain `E2E-04`–`E2E-12`, whose available unconditional machinery still stops at a conditional square-root frontier. The near-linear spine is `E2E-15`–`E2E-24`: a label-aware rank-or-decode dichotomy must retain $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ mass while producing weighted degree $D=O_\theta(d)$. The rank-reaching definition now correctly allows $D\ge d$, with $D<p$ derived downstream. The main structural simplification is `E2E-21`–`E2E-23`: lift the common cleaned relation separately on every connected component, observe that each resulting $Q_\Gamma$ contributes the divisor $Z-Q_\Gamma$ of the same polynomial $B$, and hence bound the number of distinct component polynomials by $\deg_ZB\le D/d$. Elementary edge pigeonholing then yields one global polynomial across all components, avoiding both Vinh mixing and the former standalone irreducible seed-factor lift. The target remains blocked on `E2E-15` and standalone audits of the generalized algebraic transfers; the uniform $Cd/p$ endpoint remains separately refuted.
CRITICAL: E2E-15 -> E2E-16 -> E2E-17 -> E2E-18 -> E2E-19 -> E2E-20 -> E2E-21 -> E2E-22 -> E2E-23 -> E2E-24 -> E2E-GOAL
E2E-01 [definition/candidate] deps= :: Bivariate line-versus-point instance :: Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, a function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ with $\deg P_L\le d$ for every affine line $L$. Define $$\rho=\frac d p,\qquad \epsilon=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)],\qquad \operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\max_{\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d}\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)].$$
E2E-02 [definition/candidate] deps=E2E-01 :: Accepted graph and completion predicate :: Let $$E_{\rm acc}=\{(x,L):x\in L,\ P_L(x)=f(x)\}.$$ For an accepted subgraph $G=(X,T,E)$ put $$e(G)=\frac{|E|}{p^2(p+1)}.$$ For $c>0$, let $\mathsf{Done}_c$ mean that some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ satisfies $\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c\epsilon$.
E2E-03 [reduction/candidate] deps=E2E-01 :: Fixed-exponent reduction :: For fixed $\eta>0$, put $\theta=\min\{\eta,1/4\}$. Since $0<\rho<1$, $$0<\theta\le\frac14,\qquad \rho^{1-\eta}\ge\rho^{1-\theta}.$$
E2E-04 [literature_input/candidate] deps=E2E-02 :: Accepted KTZ component measures :: Let $G=(X,T,E)$ be a connected accepted graph of minimum degree $k$, and put $\tau=|T|/[p(p+1)]$. If $$k^2\ge2^{40}dp,\qquad k^3\ge2^{40}d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\frac{|X|}{p^2}\ge\frac{k}{4p},\qquad \tau\ge\frac{k}{4p}.$$
E2E-05 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-04 :: Accepted KTZ reaching family :: Under `E2E-04`, there is $R\subseteq T$ such that $$\frac{4|T|}{k}\le|R|\le\frac{16|T|}{k},\qquad |R|\ge p+1,$$ and every $L\in T$ has more than half of its accepted neighbors incident to a line of $R\setminus\{L\}$.
E2E-06 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-05 :: Accepted KTZ weighted interpolant :: Put $$D=16\left(\left\lfloor\sqrt{d|R|}\right\rfloor+1\right).$$ There is a nonzero squarefree $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ with $\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D$, $A_Z\not\equiv0$, and $$A(M(t),P_M(t))\equiv0\qquad(M\in R).$$ Moreover, $D/k<1/5000$ and $D<k\le p<|R|$.
E2E-07 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-06 :: Accepted KTZ root extension :: For every $L\in T$, $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$
E2E-08 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-07 :: Accepted KTZ characteristic-safe cleanup :: Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content in $Z$. Then $B$ is squarefree and has total degree below $p$. There is a formal directional derivative $\mathcal D$ with $\gcd(B,\mathcal DB)=1$ and an exceptional set satisfying $$|E_{\rm exc}|\le pD^2+D|T|.$$ Outside $E_{\rm exc}$, the $B$-identity holds and $\mathcal DB$ is nonzero at every retained accepted incidence.
E2E-09 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-08 :: Accepted KTZ clean core :: One has $$\frac{|E_{\rm exc}|}{|E|}<\frac1{4000}.$$ Peeling vertices below $k_1=\lfloor k/100\rfloor$ leaves a nonempty accepted graph $G_1$ of minimum degree at least $k_1$.
E2E-10 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-09 :: Accepted KTZ simple roots :: Every point vertex $x$ of $G_1$ satisfies $$B(x,f(x))=0,\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
E2E-11 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-10 :: Accepted KTZ component decoder :: Under `E2E-04`, there is one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{k}{800p}.$$
E2E-12 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-11 :: Conditional square-root frontier :: Let $k_0=\lfloor\epsilon p/100\rfloor$. If a component of the KTZ-pruned core has line measure $\tau\le\Lambda\epsilon$ and $$\epsilon\ge\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\cdot2^{40}\Lambda}\left(\frac d p\right)^{1/2},$$ then one degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial has agreement at least $\epsilon/160000$.
E2E-13 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-02 :: Graph-only sparsification obstruction :: There are legal constant-label tables with $\epsilon\asymp1/K$ for which every family reaching half of every accepted line has size $\Omega(p/\epsilon)$ although a constant polynomial has agreement $\Omega(\epsilon)$. Moreover, at $\epsilon=d/p$ there is a connected full-support legal table with perfect decoder $Q=0$, raw accepted graph $K_{2,2}$-free, and fractional reaching optimum $$\frac{\vartheta p(p+1)}{d+1}\le\operatorname{OPT}_{\vartheta}\le\frac{\vartheta p(p+1)}{d-1}=\Theta\!\left(\frac{\vartheta p}{\epsilon}\right).$$
E2E-14 [definition/candidate] deps=E2E-02 :: Rank-reaching relation interface :: For an integer $D\ge d$, put $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\},$$ $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\bigoplus_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\qquad A\mapsto\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\bigr)_{M\in R}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching when $R\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $\operatorname{rank}\Phi_{R,D}<\dim V_D$, and every $L\in T$ has more than $D$ accepted neighbors $x$ for which some $M\in R\setminus\{L\}$ also satisfies $(x,M)\in E$.
E2E-15 [obligation/open] deps=E2E-03,E2E-14 :: Bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy :: For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$, there exist $C_\theta,K_\theta\ge1$ and $a_\theta,c_\theta>0$ such that, whenever $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\rho^{1-\theta},$$ either $\mathsf{Done}_{c_\theta}$ holds, or there is a rank-reaching tuple $(G,R,D)$ satisfying $$e(G)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,\qquad D\le K_\theta d.$$
E2E-16 [reduction/candidate] deps=E2E-15 :: Near-linear parameter and loss gate :: Enlarge $C_\theta$ until $K_\theta/C_\theta\le a_\theta/16$. In the relation branch, $$\frac{D}{\epsilon p}\le\frac{K_\theta}{C_\theta}\rho^\theta\le\frac{a_\theta}{16}.$$ Consequently, $$D<p,\qquad \left(\frac Dp\right)^2+3\frac Dp\le\frac{a_\theta\epsilon}{2}.$$
E2E-17 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-16 :: Squarefree rank-kernel relation :: For the rank-reaching tuple, there is a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ such that $$A_Z\not\equiv0,\qquad A(M(t),P_M(t))\equiv0\quad(M\in R).$$ Indeed, take the squarefree radical of a nonzero kernel element; $|R|>D$ excludes a $Z$-independent kernel element, and $D<p$ makes every nonconstant $Z$-dependence separable.
E2E-18 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-17 :: Rank-reaching root extension :: For every line vertex $L\in T$, $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$
E2E-19 [lemma/candidate] deps=E2E-18 :: Whole-relation vertical cleanup :: Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content in $Z$. Then $B$ is squarefree, primitive and $Z$-dependent, with $\operatorname{wdeg}B\le D<p$ and $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. There is $E_{\rm exc}\subseteq E$ such that $$|E_{\rm exc}|\le pD^2+D|T|.$$ Every line incident to $E\setminus E_{\rm exc}$ satisfies $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, and every retained edge $(x,L)$ satisfies $$B(x,f(x))=0,\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
E2E-20 [reduction/drafting] deps=E2E-19 :: Global simple-root peeling :: Delete $E_{\rm exc}$ and iteratively delete every point or line vertex of current degree at most $D$. The resulting nonempty accepted graph $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ satisfies $$e(G_1)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2}\epsilon,\qquad \operatorname{mindeg}(G_1)>D.$$ Every $L\in T_1$ satisfies $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, and every $x\in X_1$ satisfies $$B(x,f(x))=0,\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
E2E-21 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-20 :: Componentwise whole-relation lift :: For every connected component $\Gamma$ of $G_1$, there is $Q_\Gamma\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q_\Gamma\le d$ such that $$B(X,Y,Q_\Gamma(X,Y))\equiv0,
\qquad Q_\Gamma(x)=f(x)\quad(x\in X(\Gamma)).$$
E2E-22 [lemma/drafting] deps=E2E-21 :: Bounded component-template compression :: Let $\mathcal Q$ be the set of distinct polynomials $Q_\Gamma$ from `E2E-21`. For every $Q\in\mathcal Q$, the identity $B(X,Y,Q)=0$ implies $$Z-Q(X,Y)\mid B(X,Y,Z).$$ Distinct $Q$ give distinct monic irreducible divisors, so $$1\le|\mathcal Q|\le\deg_ZB\le\left\lfloor\frac Dd\right\rfloor\le K_\theta.$$
E2E-23 [reduction/drafting] deps=E2E-22 :: Vinh-free aggregation to one polynomial :: Partition $E_1$ by the polynomial $Q_\Gamma$ assigned to its component. Some $Q\in\mathcal Q$ carries normalized edge mass at least $$\frac{e(G_1)}{|\mathcal Q|}\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon.$$ If $X_Q$ is the union of its component point sets and $E_Q$ the corresponding edges, then $Q=f$ on $X_Q$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{|X_Q|}{p^2}\ge\frac{|E_Q|}{p^2(p+1)}\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon.$$ Together with the direct branch of `E2E-15`, this proves $\mathsf{Done}_{c_\theta^*}$ for $$c_\theta^*=\min\left\{c_\theta,\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\right\}.$$
E2E-24 [theorem/blocked] deps=E2E-23 :: Close every fixed exponent :: For every fixed $\eta>0$, there are $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\rho^{1-\eta}$$ implies that some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ satisfies $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
E2E-25 [obligation/refuted] deps=E2E-01 :: Uniform exact-linear endpoint is false :: For every absolute $C,c>0$, there are arbitrarily large primes $p$ and legal instances with $d=101$ such that $$\epsilon\ge C\frac d p,
\qquad \operatorname{Agr}_d(f)<c\epsilon.$$
E2E-26 [theorem/candidate] deps=E2E-01 :: Accepted singular-lift obstruction :: For $p>4d+1$ and $D=2d$, there is an absolutely irreducible $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree $D$ supporting more than $D$ distinct degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines, while no $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ satisfies $C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0$. Every vertically ramified lift on the supported graphs is singular.
E2E-GOAL [theorem/blocked] deps=E2E-24 :: Near-linear bivariate soundness :: For every fixed $\eta>0$ there exist $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, and every legal bivariate line table, $$\operatorname{Agr}_{\rm LVP}(f,P)\ge C_\eta\left(\frac d p\right)^{1-\eta}$$ implies that there is one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\operatorname{Agr}_{\rm LVP}(f,P).$$

reply algebraic-engine Component-template compression replaces seed-factor lift :: Round 3 can bypass the standalone seed-factor lift in `AE-19`. After `E2E-20`, apply the generic whole-relation lift componentwise. Every resulting $Q_\Gamma$ satisfies $B(X,Y,Q_\Gamma)=0$, so $Z-Q_\Gamma$ divides the common $B$; hence there are at most $\deg_ZB\le D/d$ distinct component polynomials. Edge pigeonholing then gives one global polynomial. Please audit `E2E-21`–`E2E-23` as a standalone combined lemma.
request incidence-engine Bounded-weight dichotomy still controls the exponent :: `E2E-15` remains the principal incidence obligation: retain $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ accepted mass while producing a rank-reaching relation with $D\le K_\theta d$, or decode directly. The connected carrier of researcher-0009 has optimal fractional reaching cost $\Theta(\vartheta p/\epsilon)$ and a $K_{2,2}$-free raw graph despite the perfect decoder $Q=0$, so connectivity, moments, fractional covers, and raw DRC cannot replace the label-aware alternative.
reply global Bounded weight also bounds component templates :: The bound $D=O_\theta(d)$ now has a cleaner second use. Besides making the cleanup loss $O_\theta(d/p)$ absorbable, it bounds the number of distinct component lifts by $\deg_ZB\le D/d=O_\theta(1)$. Thus `E2E-21`–`E2E-23` aggregate one polynomial by elementary edge pigeonholing and no longer require a globally heavy irreducible factor or a separate simple-seed factor-linearization theorem.

incidence-engine round 3
SUMMARY: Round 3 adds the asymmetric popularity-tail chain from researcher-0010. A high tail $u_*\epsilon^2\ge2^{70}d$ yields a two-sided core, a $\Theta(|T|/u)$ reaching sample, and one decoder with agreement $\epsilon/12800$; the central incidence obligation is therefore restricted to the complementary low-tail regime. Accepted component sampling remains a square-root-scale baseline. Exact cover constructions, bounded-order star examples, and concurrency-corrected auxiliary DRC show why graph-only selectors, fixed-order energy increments, and the displayed density-only DRC certificate cannot establish the near-linear target. The residual obligation must instead output either a decoder or a label-aware rank-reaching relation of mass $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ and weight $D=O_\theta(d)$. That bound makes cleanup loss $O_\theta(d/p)$ absorbable and leaves only $O_\theta(1)$ irreducible factors. The downstream squarefree interpolation, vertical cleanup, factor partition, seed-only factor linearization, and disconnected-factor aggregation then recover one global polynomial. The factor lift has exact revise-grade support and needs a clean standalone audit; the bounded-weight residual dichotomy is the only genuinely new mathematical bottleneck. Work states report activity only.
CRITICAL: IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH -> IE-D-RANK-REACH -> IE-D-POPULARITY-TAIL -> IE-L-TAIL-CORE -> IE-L-ASYMMETRIC-SAMPLE -> IE-T-ASYMMETRIC-DECODE -> IE-T-POPULARITY-DECODE -> IE-R-POPULARITY-SPLIT -> IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY -> IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE -> IE-L-RANK-INTERP -> IE-L-RANK-EXTEND -> IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE -> IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT -> IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN -> IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE -> IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT -> IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE -> IE-R-CLOSE -> IE-GOAL
IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH [definition/candidate] deps= :: Accepted-incidence graph :: Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, a function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and one polynomial $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$ for each affine line $L$, with a fixed bijective affine parametrization $L:\mathbb F_p\to L$. Define $$E_{\rm acc}=\{(L(t),L):P_L(t)=f(L(t))\},\qquad \epsilon=\frac{|E_{\rm acc}|}{p^2(p+1)},$$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\max_{\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d}\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)].$$ For $G=(X,T,E)\subseteq E_{\rm acc}$ put $$e(G)=\frac{|E|}{p^2(p+1)},\quad \mu(G)=\frac{|X|}{p^2},\quad \tau(G)=\frac{|T|}{p(p+1)},$$ and write $\deg_X(G)$ and $\deg_T(G)$ for its point- and line-side minimum degrees.
IE-D-RANK-REACH [definition/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Rank-reaching structured family :: For $D\ge d$ let $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\le D\}$$ and $$\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\bigoplus_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\qquad A\mapsto\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\bigr)_{M\in R}.$$ For $G=(X,T,E)$ define $$\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)=\{x:(x,L)\in E,\ \exists M\in R\setminus\{L\},\ (x,M)\in E\}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching if $R\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $$\operatorname{rank}\Phi_{R,D}<\dim V_D,$$ and $|\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>D$ for every $L\in T$.
IE-D-POPULARITY-TAIL [definition/candidate] deps=IE-D-RANK-REACH :: Accepted point-popularity tail :: Let $$a_x=|\{L:(x,L)\in E_{\rm acc}\}|,
\qquad \delta_j=\frac{1}{p^2(p+1)}\sum_{x:a_x\ge j}a_x,$$ and, when $\epsilon>0$, define $$u_*=\max\{1\le j\le p+1:\delta_j\ge\epsilon/2\}.$$
IE-L-TAIL-CORE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-POPULARITY-TAIL :: High-tail asymmetric core :: If $$u_*\epsilon^2\ge2^{70}d,$$ then the tail supported on $\{x:a_x\ge u_*\}$ contains a nonempty accepted graph $G_*$ with point minimum degree $u$, line minimum degree $v$, line measure $\tau$, and $w=\min\{u,v\}$ satisfying $$u\ge u_*/3,\qquad v\ge\epsilon p/16,$$ $$uv\ge2^{60}dp,
\qquad uw^2\ge2^{60}d\tau p^2.$$
IE-L-ASYMMETRIC-SAMPLE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-TAIL-CORE :: Near-lossless asymmetric reaching sample :: Let $G=(X,T,E)$ be nonempty with point minimum $u$, line minimum $v$, $t=|T|$, line measure $\tau$, and $w=\min\{u,v\}$. If $$uv\ge2^{60}dp,
\qquad uw^2\ge2^{60}d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\tau\ge\frac{u}{4(p+1)},\qquad \mu(G)\ge\frac{v}{4p},\qquad w^2\ge2^{57}dp,$$ and there is $R\subseteq T$ such that $$\frac{4t}{u}\le|R|\le\frac{16t}{u},\qquad |R|\ge p,$$ and $$|\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>\frac12\deg_G(L)$$ for every $L\in T$.
IE-T-ASYMMETRIC-DECODE [theorem/candidate] deps=IE-L-ASYMMETRIC-SAMPLE :: Asymmetric incidence decoder :: Under the hypotheses of `IE-L-ASYMMETRIC-SAMPLE`, there exists one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{v}{800p}.$$
IE-T-POPULARITY-DECODE [theorem/candidate] deps=IE-T-ASYMMETRIC-DECODE :: High-popularity-tail decoder :: If $$u_*\epsilon^2\ge2^{70}d,$$ then one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial satisfies $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{\epsilon}{12800}.$$
IE-R-POPULARITY-SPLIT [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-T-POPULARITY-DECODE :: Reduce to the low-tail regime :: For every instance with $\epsilon>0$, either `IE-T-POPULARITY-DECODE` applies or $$u_*\epsilon^2<2^{70}d.$$ Hence a rank-or-decode lemma proved under the latter inequality suffices for the unrestricted target.
IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY [obligation/open] deps=IE-R-POPULARITY-SPLIT :: Residual bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy :: For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$ there exist $C_\theta\ge1$, $0<a_\theta\le1$, and $K_\theta\ge1$ such that, whenever $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\theta},
\qquad u_*\epsilon^2<2^{70}d,$$ either $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,$$ or there exist $G=(X,T,E)\subseteq E_{\rm acc}$, $R\subseteq T$, and $d\le D\le K_\theta d$ such that $$e(G)\ge a_\theta\epsilon$$ and $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching.
IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY :: Near-lossless parameter gate :: Enlarge $C_\theta$ so that $C_\theta\ge16K_\theta/a_\theta$. In the structured branch, with $\rho=d/p$, $$\rho\le\frac{\epsilon}{C_\theta},
\qquad \frac Dp\le\frac{K_\theta}{C_\theta}\epsilon\le\frac{a_\theta\epsilon}{16},
\qquad D<p,$$ and therefore $$\frac{D^2}{p^2}+\frac{3D}{p}\le\frac{a_\theta\epsilon}{4}.$$
IE-L-RANK-INTERP [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE :: Low-rank squarefree interpolation :: In the structured branch there is a nonzero squarefree $A\in V_D$ such that $$A_Z\not\equiv0,
\qquad A(M(t),P_M(t))\equiv0$$ for every $M\in R$. Indeed, radicalizing a nonzero kernel element preserves these line identities; $|R|>D$ excludes a $Z$-independent radical, and $D<p$ excludes a nonconstant $p$th-power dependence on $Z$.
IE-L-RANK-EXTEND [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-RANK-INTERP :: Reached-root extension :: For every $L\in T$, the more than $D$ distinct points in $\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)$ are roots of the degree-at-most-$D$ polynomial $A(L(t),P_L(t))$. Hence $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$$ for every line vertex $L$ of $G$.
IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-RANK-EXTEND :: Primitive separable reduction :: Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content, $h=\deg H$, and $B$ is primitive in $Z$. Put $\delta=\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}B$. Then $B$ is squarefree and $Z$-dependent, $$d\le\delta,
\qquad h+\delta\le D<pd,
\qquad \gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$ At most $h$ affine lines have $H|_L\equiv0$; every other line vertex satisfies $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$.
IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE :: Weighted vertical cleanup count :: At most $$h+\left\lfloor\frac{\delta^2}{d}-\delta\right\rfloor
\le\left\lfloor\frac{D^2}{d}-D\right\rfloor$$ graph lines are content-trivial or satisfy $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. On every remaining graph line, the nonzero polynomial $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))$ has at most $\delta-d\le D-d$ roots. Deleting every nonsimple accepted incidence therefore costs at most $$\frac{\lfloor D^2/d-D\rfloor}{p(p+1)}+rac{D-d}{p}
\le\frac{D^2}{p^2}+rac Dp.$$
IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN [reduction/drafting] deps=IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT :: Near-lossless global cleanup and peeling :: After vertical cleanup, repeatedly remove each point or line vertex of current degree at most $D$. Peeling costs less than $2D/p$, so `IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE` leaves a nonempty $G_1$ satisfying $$e(G_1)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2}\epsilon,
\qquad \deg_X(G_1)>D,
\qquad \deg_T(G_1)>D.$$ Every remaining line obeys $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, and every remaining point obeys $$B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.$$
IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN :: Degree-preserving simple-root factor partition :: Factor $$B=c\prod_{i=1}^s C_i$$ into distinct irreducibles and put $\Delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i)$. Primitivity makes every $C_i$ depend on $Z$, so $$s\le\deg_ZB\le D/d\le K_\theta.$$ At every simple point exactly one factor vanishes, and every incident surviving line has the same unique factor identity. Thus $G_1$ is the disjoint union of factor classes preserving all vertex degrees. Some class $F$ and factor $C$ satisfy $$e(F)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon,
\qquad \deg_X(F),\deg_T(F)>D\ge\Delta,$$ together with $$C(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0,
\qquad C(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad C_Z(x,f(x))\ne0$$ on their respective vertices.
IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT [lemma/drafting] deps=IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE :: Seed-point factor linearization :: Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible and $Z$-dependent with $$\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}C\le D<p.$$ Suppose one accepted seed $(b,\alpha)$ satisfies $$C(b,\alpha)=0,
\qquad C_Z(b,\alpha)\ne0,$$ and is incident to more than $\Delta$ distinct affine lines, reparametrized by $L(0)=b$, such that $$P_L(0)=\alpha,
\qquad C(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$ Then there are $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ and $u\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ such that $$C=u(Z-Q).$$ Every point of the factor class in `IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE` supplies such a seed.
IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT :: Vinh-free global factor aggregation :: For the class $F$ and factor $C$ from `IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE`, $C=u(Z-Q)$ and $C(x,f(x))=0$ imply $Q(x)=f(x)$ at every point vertex of $F$. Since every affine point is incident to at most $p+1$ lines, $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{|X(F)|}{p^2}\ge e(F)
\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon.$$
IE-R-CLOSE [reduction/blocked] deps=IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE :: Close high-tail, direct, and structured branches :: Fix $\eta>0$ and put $\theta=\min\{\eta,1/4\}$. Since $0<d/p<1$, $$(d/p)^{1-\eta}\ge(d/p)^{1-\theta}.$$ Taking $C_\eta=C_\theta$ and $$c_\eta=\min\left\{\frac1{12800},a_\theta,\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\right\}$$ closes respectively the high-tail, residual direct-decoder, and structured-factor branches.
IE-GOAL [theorem/blocked] deps=IE-R-CLOSE :: Near-linear bivariate line-versus-point soundness :: For every fixed $\eta>0$ there exist $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, integer $100<d<p$, function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and degree-at-most-$d$ affine-line table, $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\eta}$$ implies the existence of one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ and $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
IE-L-POPULAR-CORE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Baseline two-sided popularity pruning :: If an accepted carrier $G$ has $e(G)=\delta>0$ and $\tau(G)\le\sigma$, then it contains $G_0$ with $$e(G_0)\ge\delta/4,
\qquad \deg_X(G_0),\deg_T(G_0)\ge\left\lfloor\frac{\delta p}{100}\right\rfloor,
\qquad \delta/4\le\tau(G_0)\le\sigma.$$
IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-RANK-REACH :: Accepted component concentration and sampling :: Let $G$ be connected with common minimum degree $k$, line measure $\tau$, and $t=|T|$. If $$k^2\ge2^{40}dp,
\qquad k^3\ge2^{40}d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\mu(G),\tau\ge\frac{k}{4p},$$ and there is $R\subseteq T$ with $$\frac{4t}{k}\le|R|\le\frac{16t}{k},
\qquad |\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>\frac12\deg_G(L)$$ for every $L\in T$.
IE-T-COMP-DECODE [theorem/candidate] deps=IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE :: Accepted component decoder :: Under `IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE`, one $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ with $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ satisfies $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{k}{800p}.$$
IE-R-SQRT-CEILING [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE :: Component-support square-root ceiling :: If $0<k\le C\epsilon p$, $\tau\ge k/(4p)$, and $$k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2,$$ then $$\epsilon^2\ge\frac{C_*}{4C^2}\frac dp.$$ Thus component-support localization alone cannot pass the square-root scale.
IE-L-TEMPLATE-POPULARITY [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Exact-template popularity decoder :: Let disjoint line families $T_1,\ldots,T_r$ carry total accepted mass $\delta$ and total line measure $\tau$, and suppose every $L\in T_j$ satisfies $$P_L(t)\equiv Q_j(L(t))$$ for some $Q_j$ of total degree at most $d$. If $$\alpha=\max_j\Pr_x[Q_j(x)=f(x)],$$ then $$\delta\le\alpha\tau+\sqrt{\frac{r\alpha\tau}{p+1}}.$$ In particular, $r\le\delta(p+1)$ implies $\alpha\ge\delta/4$.
IE-L-COVER-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Integral reaching-cover obstruction :: For $p\ge103$ and $2\le K\le p/(100\log p)$ there is a legal table with $$\frac1{2K}\le\epsilon\le\frac2K$$ such that every line subset reaching half of every accepted line has size $$|R|\ge\frac{Kp}{16}\ge\frac{p}{32\epsilon},$$ while a constant polynomial has agreement at least $\epsilon/2$.
IE-L-FRACTIONAL-COVER-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Connected fractional reaching obstruction :: There is a connected, full-support legal table with $\epsilon=d/p$, every line of accepted degree $d$, every point of accepted degree $d$ or $d+1$, and perfect decoder $Q=0$. For $0<\vartheta\le1$, let $\operatorname{OPT}_\vartheta$ be the minimum $\sum_M\lambda_M$ over $\lambda_M\ge0$ satisfying, for every line $L$, $$\sum_{x\in N(L)}\min\left\{1,\sum_{\substack{M\ne L\\x\in N(M)}}\lambda_M\right\}\ge\vartheta d.$$ Then $$\frac{\vartheta p(p+1)}{d+1}\le\operatorname{OPT}_\vartheta\le\frac{\vartheta p(p+1)}{d-1}=\Theta\left(\frac p\epsilon\right).$$
IE-L-COLLISION-IDENTITY [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH :: Exact all-order star identity :: For $2\le s\le p+1$, define the ordered distinct-line star density $$\Gamma_s=\frac{1}{p^2(p+1)_s}\sum_x(a_x)_s.$$ Then $$\Gamma_2=\epsilon^2-\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}p+rac{\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.$$
IE-L-STAR-ENERGY-OBSTRUCTION [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-COLLISION-IDENTITY :: Bounded-order star-energy obstruction :: For every fixed $S$ and all sufficiently large primes $p$, there is a legal instance with $d=101$ such that $$\epsilon=\Theta\left(\frac{\log p}{p\log\log p}\right),
\qquad \operatorname{Agr}_{101}(f)<\frac2p,$$ every affine line supports acceptance, and $$\Gamma_s=O_S(\epsilon^s)
\qquad(2\le s\le S).$$ Thus no fixed-order, scale-free star-energy increment can replace the decoder branch.
IE-L-AUXILIARY-DRC [lemma/candidate] deps=IE-L-COLLISION-IDENTITY :: Compatibility-graph DRC with concurrency correction :: Let $H$ have the $N=p(p+1)$ affine lines as vertices, joining $L\ne M$ when both accept their intersection. With $\mu=(p+1)\epsilon$ and average degree $\bar\Delta$, $$e(H)=\sum_x\binom{a_x}{2},
\qquad \bar\Delta\ge\frac{p\mu(\mu-1)}{p+1}.$$ For $1\le t\le\lfloor\bar\Delta\rfloor$, some distinct $t$-line seed has at least $$M_t(H)\ge N\frac{\binom{\lfloor\bar\Delta\rfloor}{t}}{\binom Nt}$$ common neighbors; if $\mu\ge2$ and $\bar\Delta\ge2t+2$, this is at least $N(\epsilon^2/4)^t$. Among them, at least $$M_t(H)-\frac{\binom t2(p+1)}{t-h+1}$$ meet the seed lines in at least $h$ distinct points.
IE-R-DRC-BARRIER [reduction/candidate] deps=IE-L-AUXILIARY-DRC :: Density-only DRC root-budget barrier :: Put $$D_t=16(\lfloor\sqrt{dt}\rfloor+1).$$ A transfer requiring $h>D_t$ with $h\le t$ forces $t>D_t$ and hence $t>256d$. At fixed $d,t$ and $$\epsilon=C(d/p)^{1/3},$$ the density term $N(\epsilon^2/4)^t$ tends to zero. Taking $h=D_t+1$, the concurrency correction in `IE-L-AUXILIARY-DRC` makes the resulting lower bound eventually negative, so this estimate alone supplies no root-rich target at the cubic scale.

This consumes a template partition rather than extracting it, so it complements rather than proves the bounded-weight rank alternative.

algebraic-engine round 3
SUMMARY: Round 3 keeps the bounded-weight rank-or-decode interface as the principal unresolved input but corrects its contract order: rank-reaching is defined without $D<p$, and the near-linear parameter gate derives the characteristic range. The relation branch is split into squarefree radical extraction, reached-root extension, primitive content removal, accepted weighted-resultant control of $B_Z$, and global degree-$D$ peeling. Direct vertical cleanup loses at most $(D/p)^2+3D/p$, while $D=O_\theta(d)$ also bounds the irreducible factor count by $O_\theta(1)$. The simple-seed lift is now decomposed into formal implicit uniqueness, degree-$d$ truncation, line-factor annihilation, and the evaluation-kernel argument; singular accepted examples show that $C_Z\ne0$ cannot be dropped. One factor class then aggregates across all connected components without Vinh mixing. New round-3 submissions add an asymmetric component decoder and stronger incidence-only obstructions, but neither proves the bounded-weight dichotomy. Consequently AE-12 remains open and the endpoint remains blocked pending exact standalone audits of the new algebraic packages.
CRITICAL: AE-01 -> AE-02 -> AE-03 -> AE-12 -> AE-13 -> AE-14 -> AE-15 -> AE-23 -> AE-24 -> AE-16 -> AE-17 -> AE-18 -> AE-25 -> AE-26 -> AE-19 -> AE-20 -> AE-21 -> AE-22
AE-01 [definition/candidate] deps= :: Accepted-incidence instance :: Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, a function $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and, for every affine line $L$, an affine bijection $\ell_L:\mathbb F_p\to L$ and a polynomial $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ with $\deg P_L\le d$. Define $$E_{\rm acc}=\{(\ell_L(t),L):P_L(t)=f(\ell_L(t))\},\qquad \epsilon=\frac{|E_{\rm acc}|}{p^2(p+1)},$$ and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\max_{\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d}\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)].$$
AE-02 [definition/candidate] deps=AE-01 :: Weighted restriction map :: For an integer $D\ge d$, give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$ and put $$V_D=\{A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\operatorname{wdeg}(A)\le D\},\qquad N_d(D)=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor D/d\rfloor}\binom{D-dj+2}{2}.$$ Define $$\rho_L(A)=A(\ell_L(t),P_L(t)),\qquad \Phi_{R,D}:V_D\to\prod_{M\in R}\mathbb F_p[t]_{\le D},\quad A\mapsto(\rho_M(A))_{M\in R}.$$
AE-03 [definition/candidate] deps=AE-02 :: Rank-reaching certificate :: A rank-reaching certificate is $(G,R,D)$ where $G=(X,T,E)$ is a subgraph of $E_{\rm acc}$, $R\subseteq T$, $D\in\mathbb Z$, $D\ge d$, $|R|>D$, and $$\operatorname{rank}(\Phi_{R,D})<N_d(D).$$ For every $L\in T$, it also satisfies $$\left|\left\{x:(x,L)\in E,\ \exists M\in R\setminus\{L\}\text{ with }(x,M)\in E\right\}\right|>D.$$ Put $e(G)=|E|/[p^2(p+1)]$.
AE-04 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-01 :: Constant-loss popularity pruning :: Every accepted carrier of mass $\delta>0$ contains a subgraph of mass at least $\delta/4$ and minimum degree at least $\lfloor\delta p/100\rfloor$.
AE-05 [theorem/candidate] deps=AE-01 :: Accepted component decoder :: Let $G$ be a connected accepted subgraph of minimum degree $k$ and line measure $\tau$. If $$k^2\ge2^{40}dp,
\qquad k^3\ge2^{40}d\tau p^2,$$ then some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{k}{800p}.$$
AE-06 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-05 :: Whole-explainer square-root floor :: The AE-05 construction has $$|R|=\Theta\left(\frac{\tau p^2}{k}\right),\qquad D=\Theta\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\tau p^2}{k}}\right),\qquad D<k.$$ Since $\tau\ge k/(4p)$, it requires $k^2=\Omega(dp)$ and cannot reach $k=\Theta(\epsilon p)$ below $\epsilon=\Omega((d/p)^{1/2})$.
AE-07 [theorem/candidate] deps=AE-01 :: Label-blind reaching obstructions :: There are legal instances with an $\Omega(\epsilon)$ constant decoder for which every half-reaching integral family has size $\Omega(p/\epsilon)$. There are also connected full-support instances with $\epsilon=d/p$ and $\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=1$ whose fractional reaching optimum satisfies, for $0<\theta\le1$, $$\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d+1}\le\operatorname{OPT}_\theta\le\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d-1}.$$
AE-08 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-02 :: Uniform-jet rank barrier :: Let $s,D\in\mathbb Z$ with $s\ge1$, $d\le D<p$, and $n>D$. Define $$C_s(D,d)=\sum_{a+b<s}\max\{D-a-db+1,0\}.$$ If $N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d)$ and transfer uses $h$ compatible intersections with $sh>D$, then $$h^2>\frac{dn}{12}.$$
AE-09 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-02 :: Characteristic-safe separability and weighted resultants :: If $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ is squarefree and primitive in $Z$ with $\operatorname{wdeg}(B)<pd$, then $$\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$ If coprime nonzero $U,V$ have weighted degrees $u,v$ and $Z$-degrees $r,s$, respectively, then the number of distinct degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines on which both vanish identically is at most $$su+rv-drs.$$
AE-10 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-09 :: Assigned-factor polar cleanup :: Let $B=c\prod_iC_i$ be primitive and squarefree with $\operatorname{wdeg}(B)<p$, and assign each graph line to one factor. Put $$\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),\quad r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),\quad \gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),$$ $$\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).$$ If $T_i$ is the assigned line class, then the normalized ramified accepted-incidence mass is at most $$\sum_i\frac{\min\{|T_i|,\Xi_i\}}{p(p+1)}+\sum_i\frac{|T_i|\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)},$$ where $$\gamma_i\le\delta_i-d,
\qquad \Xi_i\le\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i.$$
AE-11 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-09 :: Singular-seed obstruction :: For every $101\le d\le p-2$, there is a primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible polynomial $C$ of weighted degree $d+1$ and a lift $(b,\alpha)$ with $$C(b,\alpha)=C_Z(b,\alpha)=0$$ such that $p>d+1$ distinct degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines through $(b,\alpha)$ satisfy the $C$-identity, while no $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))=0.$$
AE-12 [obligation/open] deps=AE-03 :: Bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy :: For every fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$, there exist constants $C_\theta,K_\theta\ge1$ and $0<a_\theta,c_\theta\le1$ such that, whenever $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\theta},$$ either $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge c_\theta\epsilon,$$ or there is a rank-reaching certificate $(G,R,D)$ satisfying $$e(G)\ge a_\theta\epsilon,
\qquad d\le D\le K_\theta d.$$

AE-15 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-14 :: Reached-root identity extension :: For every line vertex $L$ of the rank-reaching graph, $$A(\ell_L(t),P_L(t))=0$$ as an identity in $\mathbb F_p[t]$.

Every $C_i$ depends on $Z$, and $$s\le\frac{\operatorname{wdeg}(B)}d\le\frac Dd\le K_\theta.$$ The vertices and edges of $G_1$ partition into factor classes $F_i$ such that every vertex retains its full $G_1$-degree, and the $i$-th class satisfies $$C_i(\ell_L(t),P_L(t))=0,
\qquad C_i(x,f(x))=0,

AE-18 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-17 :: Constant-factor concentration :: Some factor $C_i$ of weighted degree $\Delta_i$ has a class $F_i=(X_i,T_i,E_i)$ satisfying $$e(F_i)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon,
\qquad \operatorname{mindeg}(F_i)>D\ge\Delta_i.$$
AE-19 [obligation/drafting] deps=AE-26 :: Simple-seed factor linearization :: Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible and $Z$-dependent with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(C)\le D<p$. \qquad P_L(0)=\alpha,

AE-20 [reduction/candidate] deps=AE-19 :: Disconnected factor aggregation :: For the class $F_i$ from AE-18, let $C_i=u(Z-Q)$ be supplied by AE-19. Then $Q(x)=f(x)$ for every $x\in X_i$, and $$\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\ge\frac{|X_i|}{p^2}\ge\frac{|E_i|}{p^2(p+1)}=e(F_i)\ge\frac{a_\theta}{2K_\theta}\epsilon.$$
AE-21 [theorem/blocked] deps=AE-12,AE-20 :: Conditional fixed-theta algebraic engine :: For fixed $0<\theta\le1/4$, assuming the obligations in the relation branch, $$\epsilon\ge C_\theta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\theta}$$ implies that some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\theta^*\epsilon,

AE-22 [theorem/blocked] deps=AE-21 :: Near-linear algebraic propagation engine :: For every fixed $0<\eta<1$, there exist constants $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, and every accepted-incidence instance, $$\epsilon\ge C_\eta\left(\frac dp\right)^{1-\eta}$$ implies that some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c_\eta\epsilon.$$
AE-23 [lemma/drafting] deps=AE-15 :: Primitive content reduction :: Write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content in $Z$, $B$ is primitive in $Z$, $h=\deg H$, and $\delta=\operatorname{wdeg}(B)$. Then $B$ is squarefree and $Z$-dependent, $$d\le\delta,
\qquad h+\delta\le D.$$ At most $h$ line vertices satisfy $H(\ell_L(t))=0$ identically; every other line vertex satisfies $$B(\ell_L(t),P_L(t))=0.$$
AE-24 [lemma/drafting] deps=AE-09,AE-23 :: Weighted vertical ramification count :: Let $r=\deg_ZB$. The number of content-trivial or identically $B_Z$-ramified graph lines is at most $$h+(2r-1)\delta-dr^2\le h+\frac{\delta^2}{d}-\delta\le\frac{D^2}{d}-D.$$ On every other line, $B_Z(\ell_L(t),P_L(t))$ is nonzero of degree at most $\delta-d$. Deleting all accepted incidences with $B_Z=0$ has normalized mass at most $$\frac{D^2/d-D}{p(p+1)}+rac{D-d}{p}\le\left(\frac Dp\right)^2+rac Dp.$$
AE-25 [lemma/drafting] deps=AE-18 :: Characteristic-free formal implicit truncation :: Under the hypotheses of AE-19, put $$\widehat C(U,V,W)=C(b+(U,V),\alpha+W).$$ There is a unique $\Psi\in(U,V)\mathbb F_p[[U,V]]$ satisfying $$\widehat C(U,V,\Psi)=0.$$ Writing $\Psi=\sum_{j\ge1}\Psi_j$ with $\Psi_j$ homogeneous of degree $j$, define $$Q(b+(U,V))=\alpha+\sum_{j=1}^d\Psi_j(U,V).$$ Then $\deg_{\rm tot}Q\le d$ and $$Q(L(t))=P_L(t)$$ on every supplied normalized line.
AE-26 [lemma/drafting] deps=AE-25 :: Direction annihilation and evaluation kernel :: For $Q$ from AE-25, the polynomial $$R(X,Y)=C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))$$ has total degree at most $\Delta$ and vanishes identically on more than $\Delta$ distinct affine lines. Hence $R=0$. Moreover, $$\ker\bigl(\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]\xrightarrow{Z\mapsto Q}\mathbb F_p[X,Y]\bigr)=(Z-Q),$$ so irreducibility of $C$ implies $C=u(Z-Q)$ for some $u\in\mathbb F_p^\times$.
AE-27 [theorem/candidate] deps=AE-01 :: Asymmetric component decoder :: Let $G$ be a nonempty accepted subgraph with point minimum degree $u$, line minimum degree $v$, line measure $\tau$, and $w=\min\{u,v\}$. If $$uv\ge2^{60}dp,
\qquad uw^2\ge2^{60}d\tau p^2,$$ then some $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{v}{800p}.$$
AE-28 [lemma/candidate] deps=AE-01 :: Bounded-template list collapse :: Let $T_1,\ldots,T_R$ be disjoint line families and let $Q_1,\ldots,Q_R\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ have total degree at most $d$, with $$P_L(t)=Q_j(\ell_L(t))\qquad(L\in T_j).$$ Let $\delta$ be the normalized accepted mass on $\bigcup_jT_j$, let $\tau=|\bigcup_jT_j|/[p(p+1)]$, and put $\alpha=\max_j\Pr_x[Q_j(x)=f(x)]$. Then $$\delta\le\alpha\tau+\sqrt{\frac{R\alpha\tau}{p+1}}.$$ In particular, $R\le\delta(p+1)$ implies $\alpha\ge\delta/4$.

The dichotomy returns $d\le D\le K_\theta d$, and only the downstream parameter gate derives $D<p$. Please align `IE-D-RANK-REACH` and `E2E-14` with AE-03--AE-13 to remove the contract-order circularity.

Polynomial division then gives $C=u(Z-Q)$. AE-20 keeps disconnected aggregation separate.
reply global Use weighted direct-B_Z cleanup :: The bounded-weight branch can use direct vertical cleanup rather than a generic polar. AE-23--AE-24 give at most $D^2/d-D$ content-or-identically-ramified lines and at most $D-d$ bad parameters on every other line. Together with degree-$D$ peeling, AE-16 loses at most $(D/p)^2+3D/p$. This specializes the accepted separability and weighted-resultant steps `researcher-0006:P1.1/P2.1`.
idea end-to-end-soundness Bounded-template aggregation fallback :: As an optional list-collapse fallback, `researcher-0010:[P9].1` shows that $R$ global templates carrying accepted mass $\delta$ yield one template of agreement at least $\delta/4$ when $R\le\delta(p+1)$. Thus, if every bounded-weight factor class is linearized, AE-28 can aggregate the resulting $O_\theta(1)$ templates. Its enclosing audit is still `revise`, so AE-20's elementary heavy-class aggregation remains the primary path.

/bin/zsh -lc "sed -n '1,220p' dashboard/scripts/validate-roadmap-math.mjs" in /Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point
 succeeded in 0ms:
import fs from 'node:fs';
import katex from 'katex';

const path = process.argv[2] ?? 'public/research-data.json';
const data = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8'));
const roadmaps = data.proof_roadmaps?.roadmaps ?? (data.nodes ? [data] : []);
const messages = data.message_board?.messages ?? data.messages ?? [];
const failures = [];
let mathSegments = 0;

function validateMarkdown(markdown, reference) {
  const value = String(markdown ?? '');
  const control = Array.from(value).find((character) => {
    const code = character.charCodeAt(0);
    return code < 32 && character !== '\n' && character !== '\t';
  });
  if (control) failures.push(`${reference}: contains a control character`);
  const mathPattern = /\$\$([\s\S]*?)\$\$|\$([^$\n]+?)\$/g;
  const prose = value.replace(mathPattern, (_, display, inline) => {
    const expression = display ?? inline ?? '';
    mathSegments += 1;
    try {
      katex.renderToString(expression, { throwOnError: true, strict: 'warn' });
    } catch (error) {
      failures.push(`${reference}: ${error.message}`);
    }
    return ' ';
  });
  if ((value.match(/\$/g) ?? []).length % 2 !== 0) {
    failures.push(`${reference}: unbalanced math delimiter`);
  }
  if (/\\[A-Za-z]+/.test(prose)) {
    failures.push(`${reference}: TeX command outside math delimiters`);
  }
}

for (const roadmap of roadmaps) {
  validateMarkdown(roadmap.target_statement, `${roadmap.roadmap_id} target`);
  for (const node of roadmap.nodes ?? []) {
    validateMarkdown(node.statement_markdown, `${roadmap.roadmap_id}:${node.id}`);
  }
}
for (const message of messages) {
  validateMarkdown(message.body_markdown, `${message.id ?? 'draft-message'} body`);
}

if (failures.length) {
  process.stderr.write(`${failures.join('\n')}\n`);
  process.exit(1);
}
process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify({ passed: true, roadmaps: roadmaps.length, messages: messages.length, math_segments: mathSegments })}\n`);
